## Week 5 — Execution engine + scheduling

**Milestone:** Async runs with live progress across all datasource types; scheduling operational

### Async execution backend
- [ ] Celery + Redis background task runner for GX scan execution
- [ ] Run progress API — poll endpoint returning per-check live status
- [ ] Cancel run endpoint — gracefully terminate in-progress Celery task
- [ ] Run history retention policy — configurable purge of results older than N days
- [ ] Flat file run path — resolve batch, load via Pandas, execute GX suite
- [ ] UC run path — submit job to Databricks SQL Warehouse, execute GX suite

### ADF hybrid monitoring (webhook + polling fallback)
- [ ] Celery beat task — poll ADF REST API every 10 min for **succeeded** pipeline runs and full run metadata; skip pipelines already updated by webhook within the last 10 min to avoid redundant calls
- [ ] Gap recovery logic — on startup and on a 30-min schedule, fetch all ADF run statuses for the past hour and upsert any missed events (catches webhook failures during API restarts or deploys)
- [ ] `GET /api/v1/adf/pipelines` — return latest status per pipeline (source: DB, updated by both webhook and polling); used by the UI for display

> **Design note:** Webhook handles failure events near-instantly (~1–5 min). Polling handles succeeded runs, run detail enrichment, and recovery from missed webhook calls. The UI always reads from the DB — it never calls ADF directly.

### Execution UI
- [ ] Run now panel — suite picker, env / datasource, notification target
- [ ] Live run progress UI — check-by-check status with spinner + cancel button
- [ ] Scheduled runs table — create, pause, delete cron schedules
- [ ] Recent runs audit table with drill-down link to results

---

## Week 6 — Results dashboard + alerting

**Milestone:** Full results dashboard live across all source types; alerts firing with suppression

### Results dashboard
- [ ] Health score stat cards + 7-day trend chart
- [ ] Per-suite pass / fail progress bars — updated to show warn / fail / critical breakdown
- [ ] Results filter bar — by env, datasource type, suite, date range, status (including warn / fail / critical)
- [ ] Failed check drill-down — sample failing rows from GX result
- [ ] Per-check historical trend chart (not just overall health score)
- [ ] ADF pipeline status panel — polls `GET /api/v1/adf/pipelines` every 30s; status reflects webhook-pushed failures (near real-time) and polling-filled succeeded runs; shows pipeline name, last run time, duration, status badge, and correlated DQ result
- [ ] Datasource type filter — toggle Snowflake / flat file / Unity Catalog on results view
- [ ] CSV + PDF export of results
- [ ] Severity badge colours — green (pass), amber (warn), red (fail), dark red (critical); applied consistently across all result tables, drill-downs, and suite cards
- [ ] Health score weighting — apply agreed penalty weights (warn / fail / critical) in health score calculation; show severity breakdown in the 7-day trend tooltip

### Alerting
- [ ] Notification config UI — Teams webhook per suite, alert on fail / warn / always
- [ ] Alert suppression / snooze — silence a specific check for N hours
- [ ] Alert dedup — fire on first failure only, not on every subsequent scheduled run
- [ ] Teams adaptive card payload — check name, datasource, table / file, observed vs expected
- [ ] Severity-aware alert routing — `warn` fires a quieter notification (different card colour, no @mention); `fail` fires standard alert; `critical` fires with @channel mention and distinct card styling

---

## Week 5 — Execution engine + scheduling

**Exit gate:** Async runs with live progress across all datasource types; scheduling operational.

### Async execution backend (9 tasks — 8/9 ✅)
- [x] ✅ Celery + Redis background task runner for GX scan execution — `run_suite` task + `run_service` — landed early via [PR 4a](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/pull/74) + [PR 4c-i](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/pull/78) + [PR 4c-ii](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/pull/79)
- [x] ✅ Generalise `run_suite` worker dispatch — select the `CheckRunner` by `connection.type` via the runner registry (`build_check_runner`), replacing the Snowflake-hardcoded wiring in `worker/tasks.py`; the seam that makes the flat-file / UC run paths route correctly and post-v1 RDBMS adapters a drop-in — [PR #146-fix](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/issues/146) _(added per [ADR 0011](adr/0011-extensibility-seams-for-deferred-integrations.md); profiler dispatch collapsed onto a parallel registry in the same effort, [#147](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/issues/147))_
- [x] ✅ **Check target table/path** — modeled as a **per-suite** datasource-shaped `Suite.target` (JSONB, shaped like the column-profiler request: `table`/`schema`/`catalog`/`path`/`file_format`), since `execute_run` runs all of a suite's checks against one batch (the GX data-asset-per-suite shape). `services/run_target.resolve_target` resolves it per connection type to the runner's `(table, schema, catalog)` triple (flat-file path rides the table-shaped `CheckRunner`'s `table` slot); `validate_target` is the write-time 422 guard on suite create/update. Migration `b1f2c3d4e5a6` (additive nullable column, tested up/down on Postgres). **Dispatch ungated**: `_trigger_suites` now hands each created run to `run_suite.delay` (lazy import — worker↔service cycle), broker-fail marks the run `failed`; `run_suite` resolves the target from the suite (targetless → clean `failed`), so the worker no longer takes a `table` arg; stale `until_week3` wording removed. 13 resolver units + worker/orchestration/suite-API target tests. _(Re-tracked from Week 3; [#215](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/issues/215); PR-C0a)_
- [x] ✅ **Manual run trigger + run/result read API** — `POST /suites/{id}/run` queues a `Run` and dispatches it via the shared `run_dispatch.dispatch_run` helper (**edit**-gated — the capability ladder grants "trigger runs" at edit); the suite target (#215) is resolved **up front** so a targetless/misconfigured suite fails fast with 422 instead of a queued→failed run, and a broker outage marks the run `failed` + returns 503 (same contract as the probe). New `api/v1/runs.py` read surface for the results page: `GET /runs?suite_id=&status=&limit=` and `GET /runs/{id}` (run + joined `Result`s) are **suite-scoped** — the list filters to accessible suites and the detail gates on `require_permission(view)`, so per-suite sharing + existence-hiding hold (exactly why direct-Postgres/Grafana is rejected as the primary surface); `GET /pipeline_runs?provider=&status=` is the orchestration monitoring feed (auth-only, not suite-scoped). Read helpers live in `run_service.list_runs/get_run/list_results` + `orchestration_service.list_pipeline_runs`. 14 DB-backed API tests (trigger happy/targetless-422/edit-403/no-access-404/broker-503; list scoping+filters+limit; detail+results / 404s; pipeline filters + auth). _(Run-enablement gap; PR-C0b; gates the Week-6 Results page PR-C1)_
- [x] ✅ Run progress API — poll endpoint returning per-check live status — `GET /runs/{id}/progress` (suite-scoped): run lifecycle status + per-check resolution (status or null=pending) + a status histogram, the compact shape the live-progress UI polls. DB-driven (worker writes status + result rows); GX's atomic batch means per-check rows land at completion, so this reports lifecycle + final resolution (documented). **[PR #301](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/pull/301)** (A1)
- [x] ✅ Cancel run endpoint — gracefully terminate in-progress Celery task — `POST /runs/{id}/cancel` (edit-gated, suite-scoped, 409 if terminal): marks `cancelled` + best-effort `revoke` of the captured `runs.celery_task_id` (migration `d5e6f7a8b9c0`). Cooperative — no SIGKILL mid-GX: worker skips a cancelled run at pickup + `execute_run` won't overwrite a mid-run cancel. Deterministic for queued, best-effort for in-flight. Folded **#227** (one dispatch-failure shape in `run_dispatch`, aligning probe's `finished_at`). **[PR #302](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/pull/302)** (A2)
- [ ] ⬜ Run history retention policy — configurable purge of results older than N days
- [x] ✅ Flat file run path — resolve batch, load via Pandas, execute GX suite — batch-aware target (regex `pattern` + `latest`/`specific` strategy + `prefix`) resolved live via `run_target.materialize_path` → `flatfile.resolve_batch_file` (Week-3 seam); literal `{path}` unchanged. The pandas load + GX execution already lived in `FlatFileCheckRunner` (Week 3). **[PR #298](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/pull/298)** (A4)
- [x] ✅ UC run path — submit job to Databricks SQL Warehouse, execute GX suite — `UnityCatalogCheckRunner` reads the target table via the Databricks SQL Warehouse into a pandas DataFrame + runs GX in-process (DQX swap-in shape, §5), wired through the runner registry (#146) + per-suite target (#215); shipped Week 3, worker catalog-threading coverage closed in A5. Live SQL-Warehouse read is the deferred smoke. **[PR #299](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/pull/299)** (A5)

> **Operational result statuses ([#122](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/issues/122) — DONE, closed):** both halves shipped. **`error`** ([PR #297](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/pull/297)) — a check GX can't *evaluate* (per-expectation exception, e.g. a missing column) persists as `error` (no severity, no `metric_value`) instead of being conflated with a data `fail`, never failing its siblings or the run. **`skip`** ([PR #298](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/pull/298), with the flat-file batch run path) — a genuinely-absent batch maps `BatchNotFoundError` → `run_service.skip_run` (every check `skip`, run still succeeds), not a failure. Both values were already in the `RESULT_STATUSES` CHECK (Week-3 schema seam); these emit them. Unit + real-GX + Postgres-integration tests for each.

### ADF + Airflow hybrid monitoring (webhook + polling fallback) (4 tasks — 4/4 ✅)
- [x] ✅ Celery beat task — poll ADF REST API every 10 min for **succeeded** runs (skip pipelines updated by webhook recently) — provider-agnostic `poll_orchestration_runs` beat task through the `OrchestrationProvider` seam — [PR #171](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/pull/189)
- [x] ✅ Celery beat task — poll Airflow REST API `dagRuns` every 10 min — same provider-agnostic beat task (ADF + Airflow share it) — [PR #171](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/pull/189) _(added per ADR 0004; not in roadmap)_
- [x] ✅ Gap recovery logic — on startup + every 30 min, fetch last hour of run statuses — provider-agnostic `recover_orchestration_gaps` beat task reuses the `_poll_orchestration_runs` pipeline with a 1-hour `lookback` (vs the 10-min poll's 15-min window); idempotent upsert + `skip_updated_since` make overlap harmless. New 30-min beat entry + a one-off on worker boot via the `worker_ready` signal (enqueued by name, broker-failure swallowed). **[PR #307](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/pull/307)** (B2)
- [x] ✅ `GET /api/v1/orchestration/pipelines` — latest status per pipeline/DAG, provider-agnostic — one row per distinct `(provider, pipeline_or_dag_id, env)` carrying its most-recent run, via Postgres `DISTINCT ON` (no provider branching); auth-only gated (monitoring, not suite-scoped); `provider`/`env`/`limit` filters. Recency = `COALESCE(started_at, created_at)` so a fresh failure event with no start time isn't masked by an older fully-timed run (`/code-review` catch). **[PR #305](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/pull/305)** (B1); follow-up [#306](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/issues/306) (validate provider/env → 422 on both read endpoints)

> **`trigger_bindings` CRUD** (provider-agnostic suite-run triggers — `provider`/`pipeline_or_dag_id`/`env` → `suite_id`) landed early via [PR #172](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/pull/190), unblocking the trigger-on-success path skeletoned in Week 2 PR 8.

### Execution UI (5 tasks — 1/5 ✅)
- [ ] ⬜ Run now panel — suite picker, env / datasource, notification target
- [ ] ⬜ Live run progress UI — check-by-check status with spinner + cancel button
- [ ] ⬜ Scheduled runs table — create, pause, delete cron schedules
- [ ] ⬜ Recent runs audit table with drill-down link to results
- [x] ✅ **Suite Triggers panel** — UI over the `trigger_bindings` CRUD (#172): bind/unbind a pipeline/DAG (`provider` + `pipeline_or_dag_id` + `env`) to a suite so it runs on the pipeline's success. **Suite-level**, not a check field (orchestration providers are never a datasource/check attribute, CLAUDE.md §4); inline `TriggersPanel` card on the suite detail — list + enable/disable toggle + remove + add form (ADF/Airflow · pipeline-or-DAG id · env), edit-gated (read-only for viewers). `api/triggerBindings.ts` + 6 component tests. _(PR #247; closes [#216](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/issues/216))_

**Week 5 total: 13 / 18** _(early credit: Celery task runner in PR 4; worker runner-dispatch #146; ADF + Airflow 10-min polling #171; plus `trigger_bindings` CRUD #172 out-of-band; check target-table + dispatch ungate #215 (PR-C0a); manual run trigger + run/result read API (PR-C0b); **Suite Triggers UI #216 (PR #247)**. **This stretch (Phase 1–3):** `error` status #297, flat-file batch + `skip` #298, UC run-path coverage #299, run progress API #301, cancel run + #227 #302, `GET /orchestration/pipelines` #305, gap-recovery beat #307. +3 tasks re-tracked here: check target-table #215 (slipped from W3), Suite Triggers UI #216, and the run-enablement read API (PR-C0b). The **manual Run button** (PR #245) + the **run-target editor** (PR #246) also shipped earlier this week. **Remaining (5):** run-history retention, run-now panel, live run-progress UI, scheduled-runs table, recent-runs audit table — plus the scheduling backend (A7))_

```
1	"""Celery application for DataQ's async execution backbone.
2	
3	GX suite runs are dispatched from FastAPI and executed here so the request
4	thread returns immediately (run created as ``queued``, worker drives it to
5	``running`` → ``succeeded``/``failed``).
6	
7	The CLAUDE.md observability rule requires ``request_id`` correlation to flow
8	FastAPI → Celery → GX. We carry it on the task message headers: the caller
9	injects the active request_id on publish, and the worker restores it into the
10	same ``request_id_var`` ContextVar the structlog processor chain reads from, so
11	worker log lines correlate with the request that triggered them.
12	"""
13	
14	from typing import Any
15	
16	from celery import Celery
17	from celery.signals import (
18	    beat_init,
19	    before_task_publish,
20	    setup_logging,
21	    task_postrun,
22	    task_prerun,
23	)
24	
25	from backend.app.core.config import get_settings
26	from backend.app.core.logging import configure_logging, get_logger, request_id_var
27	
28	# Message-header key carrying the originating request_id across the broker.
29	REQUEST_ID_HEADER = "request_id"
30	# Attribute on task.request where prerun stashes the ContextVar reset handle
31	# for postrun. Deliberately avoids the word "token" so it isn't mistaken for a
32	# secret by Bandit / Ruff (B105 / S105).
33	_REQUEST_ID_RESET_ATTR = "_dataq_request_id_reset"
34	
35	
36	def create_celery_app() -> Celery:
37	    settings = get_settings()
38	    app = Celery(
39	        "dataq",
40	        broker=settings.redis_url,
41	        backend=settings.redis_url,
42	    )
43	    app.conf.update(
44	        task_serializer="json",
45	        result_serializer="json",
46	        accept_content=["json"],
47	        timezone="UTC",
48	        enable_utc=True,
49	        # Surface a 'started' state so the run-status read-back can distinguish
50	        # queued from running without waiting for completion.
51	        task_track_started=True,
52	        # Celery-beat schedule. The orchestration polling fallback (#171) runs
53	        # every 10 min as the success channel for runs that produced no webhook;
54	        # the task looks back further than the interval so nothing slips the gap.
55	        # Beat runs embedded in the dev worker (`worker -B`); prod uses a separate
56	        # beat process.
57	        # Gap recovery (B2) sweeps a wider 1-hour window every 30 min (plus once
58	        # on beat startup, via the beat_init signal below) to re-ingest runs
59	        # missed while the system was down — idempotent with the 10-min poll.
60	        beat_schedule={
61	            "poll-orchestration-runs": {
62	                "task": "poll_orchestration_runs",
63	                "schedule": 600.0,  # 10 minutes
64	            },
65	            "recover-orchestration-gaps": {
66	                "task": "recover_orchestration_gaps",
67	                "schedule": 1800.0,  # 30 minutes
68	            },
69	        },
70	    )
71	    # Register task modules on worker boot (looks for backend.app.worker.tasks).
72	    app.autodiscover_tasks(["backend.app.worker"])
73	    return app
74	
75	
76	celery_app = create_celery_app()
77	
78	
79	@setup_logging.connect  # type: ignore[untyped-decorator]  # celery signal .connect is unannotated
80	def _configure_celery_logging(**_kwargs: Any) -> None:
81	    """Disable Celery's default logging and route worker logs through structlog.
82	
83	    Connecting any receiver to ``setup_logging`` tells Celery not to configure
84	    logging itself, so our JSON + PII-redacting processor chain stays in force
85	    inside the worker exactly as it is in the API.
86	    """
87	    configure_logging()
88	
89	
90	@before_task_publish.connect  # type: ignore[untyped-decorator]  # celery signal .connect is unannotated
91	def _inject_request_id(headers: dict[str, Any] | None = None, **_kwargs: Any) -> None:
92	    """Caller side (FastAPI): stamp the active request_id onto task headers."""
93	    if headers is None:
94	        return
95	    rid = request_id_var.get()
96	    if rid is not None:
97	        headers[REQUEST_ID_HEADER] = rid
98	
99	
100	@task_prerun.connect  # type: ignore[untyped-decorator]  # celery signal .connect is unannotated
101	def _restore_request_id(task: Any = None, **_kwargs: Any) -> None:
102	    """Worker side: restore request_id from the message into the ContextVar.
103	
104	    Custom headers added in ``before_task_publish`` are exposed as attributes on
105	    ``task.request`` under the protocol-v2 message format. We stash the reset
106	    token on ``task.request`` so ``task_postrun`` can restore the *prior* value
107	    rather than blindly clearing — under ``task_always_eager`` these signals run
108	    in the caller's context, so a blanket reset would drop the request_id for
109	    the rest of the request handler.
110	    """
111	    rid = getattr(task.request, REQUEST_ID_HEADER, None) if task is not None else None
112	    if rid and task is not None:
113	        token = request_id_var.set(rid)
114	        setattr(task.request, _REQUEST_ID_RESET_ATTR, token)
115	
116	
117	@task_postrun.connect  # type: ignore[untyped-decorator]  # celery signal .connect is unannotated
118	def _clear_request_id(task: Any = None, **_kwargs: Any) -> None:
119	    """Worker side: restore the ContextVar to its pre-task value.
120	
121	    Only resets when ``task_prerun`` actually set it (token present), mirroring
122	    the ``reset(token)`` pattern used by ``request_id_middleware`` in main.py.
123	    """
124	    token = getattr(task.request, _REQUEST_ID_RESET_ATTR, None) if task is not None else None
125	    if token is not None:
126	        request_id_var.reset(token)
127	
128	
129	@beat_init.connect  # type: ignore[untyped-decorator]  # celery signal .connect is unannotated
130	def _recover_gaps_on_beat_start(**_kwargs: Any) -> None:
131	    """When the beat scheduler starts, kick a one-off gap recovery (B2).
132	
133	    Tied to ``beat_init`` (one beat process per deployment) rather than worker
134	    boot, so it fires **once** per restart instead of once per worker — no
135	    thundering herd of identical sweeps on a multi-worker deploy. Catches runs
136	    that completed while the system was down; the 30-min beat alone would leave
137	    that window unswept until its first tick. Enqueued by name (decoupled from
138	    the task module) to the broker so a ready worker runs it. Best-effort: a
139	    broker hiccup at startup must not crash beat (the schedule recovers shortly).
140	    """
141	    try:
142	        celery_app.send_task("recover_orchestration_gaps")
143	    except Exception:  # pragma: no cover - defensive; startup must not fail on broker
144	        get_logger(__name__).exception("gap_recovery_startup_dispatch_failed")
145

1	"""Dispatch a persisted `Run` to the Celery worker — the one place that publishes.
2	
3	Publishes ``run_suite`` **by name** via ``celery_app.send_task`` rather than
4	importing the task object. That decoupling is deliberate: ``worker.tasks``
5	imports service modules (e.g. ``orchestration_service``), so a service importing
6	``worker.tasks`` back would be a cyclic import (CodeQL `py/cyclic-import`). By
7	name there is no import edge service → worker, and the broker resolves the task
8	on the worker side. The ``before_task_publish`` signal still fires, so the
9	``request_id`` correlation header is carried exactly as for ``.delay``.
10	
11	Raises on a broker/publish failure; the caller owns the policy for a stuck run
12	(the probe endpoint surfaces 503; the pipeline-trigger path marks the run
13	``failed`` so it isn't left ``queued``).
14	"""
15	
16	from __future__ import annotations
17	
18	import uuid
19	from datetime import UTC, datetime
20	
21	from backend.app.core.logging import get_logger
22	from backend.app.db.models import Run
23	from backend.app.worker.celery_app import celery_app
24	
25	log = get_logger(__name__)
26	
27	_RUN_SUITE_TASK = "run_suite"
28	
29	
30	def dispatch_run(run_id: uuid.UUID) -> str:
31	    """Publish the ``run_suite`` task for ``run_id`` and return its Celery task id.
32	
33	    The task id is stored on the `Run` (``celery_task_id``) so a later cancel can
34	    revoke a still-queued task. Raises if the broker is down — the caller owns the
35	    policy for the stuck run (`mark_dispatch_failed` + 503 / log).
36	
37	    No 2-phase commit spans the broker and the DB: if the publish succeeds but the
38	    caller's follow-up commit of ``celery_task_id`` fails (a rare DB blip in that
39	    window), the task still runs — the worker just can't be revoked by id and falls
40	    back to the cooperative ``cancelled``-status check. Self-correcting and benign.
41	    """
42	    result = celery_app.send_task(_RUN_SUITE_TASK, args=[str(run_id)])
43	    return str(result.id)
44	
45	
46	def mark_dispatch_failed(run: Run) -> None:
47	    """The canonical terminal-failed shape for a broker/dispatch failure.
48	
49	    One definition shared by every trigger path (probe, manual run, pipeline
50	    success) so a never-dispatched run is recorded identically everywhere:
51	    ``failed`` with ``finished_at`` set and ``started_at`` left as-is (NULL — it
52	    never started), keeping run-history / duration views consistent (#227).
53	    """
54	    run.status = "failed"
55	    run.finished_at = datetime.now(UTC)
56	
57	
58	def revoke_run(task_id: str | None) -> None:
59	    """Best-effort revoke of a dispatched run's Celery task.
60	
61	    Drops the task if it's still **queued** (not yet picked up). Deliberately no
62	    ``terminate`` — we don't SIGKILL a worker mid-GX (it would take out sibling
63	    tasks); an already-running task is stopped **cooperatively** (the worker
64	    checks for a ``cancelled`` run status). A no-op for an un-dispatched run
65	    (``task_id is None``); broker errors are swallowed (the DB status is already
66	    ``cancelled`` and the worker's cooperative check still applies).
67	    """
68	    if not task_id:
69	        return
70	    try:
71	        celery_app.control.revoke(task_id)
72	    except Exception:
73	        log.warning("run_revoke_failed", celery_task_id=task_id)
74

172	    suite_id: uuid.UUID,
173	    current_user: Annotated[User, Depends(get_current_user)],

180	# ───────────────────────── manual run trigger ──────────────────────

182	
183	@router.post(
184	    "/suites/{suite_id}/run",
185	    response_model=RunRead,
186	    status_code=status.HTTP_202_ACCEPTED,
187	    summary="Trigger a run of the suite",
188	)
189	def trigger_suite_run(
190	    suite_id: uuid.UUID,
191	    current_user: Annotated[User, Depends(get_current_user)],
192	    db: Annotated[Session, Depends(get_db)],
193	) -> RunRead:
194	    """Queue a run of the suite and dispatch it to the worker.
195	
196	    `edit` gates this (the capability ladder grants 'trigger runs' at edit). The
197	    suite's target (#215) is resolved up front so a targetless/misconfigured
198	    suite fails fast with a 422 instead of a queued→failed run the caller has to
199	    poll to discover. A broker outage marks the run `failed` (never left stuck
200	    `queued`) and surfaces 503 — the same contract as the probe endpoint.
201	    """
202	    suite = require_permission(db, suite_id, current_user.id, minimum="edit")
203	    connection = db.get(Connection, suite.connection_id)
204	    assert connection is not None  # FK is RESTRICT; a suite always has its connection
205	    # Raises SuiteTargetInvalidError (422) for a targetless / wrong-datasource target.
206	    run_target.resolve_target(connection.type, suite.target)
207	
208	    run = Run(suite_id=suite.id, status="queued", triggered_by=f"manual:{current_user.id}")
209	    db.add(run)
210	    db.commit()
211	    db.refresh(run)
212	
213	    try:
214	        run.celery_task_id = run_dispatch.dispatch_run(run.id)
215	        db.commit()
216	    except Exception as exc:  # broker unreachable — don't leave the run stuck queued
217	        # Canonical terminal-failed shape shared across all trigger paths (#227):
218	        # finished_at set, started_at NULL (it never started).
219	        run_dispatch.mark_dispatch_failed(run)
220	        db.commit()
221	        raise HTTPException(
222	            status_code=status.HTTP_503_SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE,
223	            detail="failed to dispatch run",
224	        ) from exc
225	    return RunRead.model_validate(run)
226	
227	
228	# ───────────────────────── export / import (portable documents) ─────
229

183	def _trigger_suites(
184	    session: Session, *, provider: str, connection: Connection, update: RunUpdate
185	) -> list[Run]:
186	    """Create one queued `Run` per enabled `trigger_binding` for a succeeded run.
187	
188	    Idempotent on the ``triggered_by`` marker ``<provider>:<pipeline>:<run_id>``:
189	    a replayed event (or a webhook + poll double-delivery) does not spawn a
190	    second run for the same (suite, pipeline-run).
191	
192	    Each created run is handed to Celery (``run_suite``) once committed; the
193	    worker resolves the suite's target (#215) and fails the run cleanly if the
194	    suite is targetless. A broker failure marks that run ``failed`` rather than
195	    leaving it stuck ``queued`` (the 10-min poll won't re-dispatch a stale row).
196	    """
197	    marker = f"{provider}:{update.pipeline_or_dag_id}:{update.provider_run_id}"
198	    bindings = list(
199	        session.scalars(
200	            select(TriggerBinding).where(
201	                TriggerBinding.provider == provider,
202	                TriggerBinding.pipeline_or_dag_id == update.pipeline_or_dag_id,
203	                TriggerBinding.env == connection.env,
204	                TriggerBinding.enabled.is_(True),
205	            )
206	        )
207	    )
208	    created: list[Run] = []
209	    for binding in bindings:
210	        already = session.scalar(
211	            select(Run.id).where(Run.suite_id == binding.suite_id, Run.triggered_by == marker)
212	        )
213	        if already is not None:
214	            continue
215	        run = Run(suite_id=binding.suite_id, status="queued", triggered_by=marker)
216	        session.add(run)
217	        created.append(run)
218	
219	    if created:
220	        session.commit()
221	        for run in created:
222	            session.refresh(run)
223	        log.info(
224	            "suite_runs_triggered",
225	            provider=provider,
226	            pipeline=update.pipeline_or_dag_id,
227	            run_marker=marker,
228	            count=len(created),
229	        )
230	        for run in created:
231	            try:
232	                run.celery_task_id = run_dispatch.dispatch_run(run.id)
233	                session.commit()
234	            except Exception:
235	                # Broker down: don't leave the run stuck 'queued'. Canonical
236	                # terminal-failed shape shared across all trigger paths (#227).
237	                run_dispatch.mark_dispatch_failed(run)
238	                session.commit()
239	                log.exception("suite_dispatch_failed", run_id=str(run.id))
240	    return created
241	
242	
```

1	"""Trigger-binding CRUD endpoints — manage suite-run triggers (provider-agnostic).
2	
3	Thin HTTP layer over `trigger_binding_service`: a binding maps a successful
4	orchestrator run (`provider`/`pipeline_or_dag_id`/`env`) to a `suite_id`. All
5	validation, suite-permission gating, and conflict handling live in the service.
6	"""

1	"""Trigger-binding CRUD — provider-agnostic management of the suite-run triggers.
2	
3	A `trigger_binding` maps a successful orchestrator run to a suite execution:
4	composite key (`provider`, `pipeline_or_dag_id`, `env`) → `suite_id` (ADR 0004).
5	The webhook + polling paths (`orchestration_service`) *consume* enabled bindings;
6	this module lets users *manage* them.
7	
8	Provider-agnostic by design (CLAUDE.md §10): `provider` is validated against the
9	shared `ORCHESTRATION_PROVIDERS` set — there is no ADF-specific table or branch.
10	Because a binding automates a suite, management is gated on the caller's suite
11	permission (`suite_authz.require_permission`): `edit` to create / change / delete,
12	`view` to read — so you can't wire a pipeline to a suite you can't access.
13	
14	FastAPI-free (like the other services): takes a `Session`, returns ORM models,
15	raises typed `DataQError`s the envelope maps to status codes.
16	"""
17	
18	from __future__ import annotations
19	
20	import uuid
21	
22	from sqlalchemy import or_, select
23	from sqlalchemy.exc import IntegrityError
24	from sqlalchemy.orm import Session
25	
26	from backend.app.core.errors import DataQError
27	from backend.app.core.logging import get_logger
28	from backend.app.db.models import ENVS, ORCHESTRATION_PROVIDERS, Share, Suite, TriggerBinding
29	from backend.app.services.suite_authz import require_permission
30	
31	log = get_logger(__name__)
32	
33	
34	class TriggerBindingNotFoundError(DataQError):
35	    status_code = 404
36	    code = "trigger_binding_not_found"
37	
38	
39	class TriggerBindingInvalidError(DataQError):
40	    status_code = 422
41	    code = "trigger_binding_invalid"
42	
43	
44	class TriggerBindingConflictError(DataQError):
45	    status_code = 409
46	    code = "trigger_binding_conflict"
47	
48	
49	def _validate_provider_env(provider: str, env: str) -> None:
50	    if provider not in ORCHESTRATION_PROVIDERS:
51	        raise TriggerBindingInvalidError(
52	            f"invalid provider {provider!r}",
53	            detail={"allowed": list(ORCHESTRATION_PROVIDERS)},
54	        )
55	    if env not in ENVS:
56	        raise TriggerBindingInvalidError(f"invalid env {env!r}", detail={"allowed": list(ENVS)})
57	
58	
59	def create_binding(
60	    session: Session,
61	    *,
62	    provider: str,
63	    pipeline_or_dag_id: str,
64	    env: str,
65	    suite_id: uuid.UUID,
66	    user_id: uuid.UUID,
67	    enabled: bool = True,
68	) -> TriggerBinding:
69	    """Create a binding. Requires `edit` on the target suite (404/403 otherwise).
70	
71	    The composite key (`provider`, `pipeline_or_dag_id`, `env`, `suite_id`) is
72	    unique — a duplicate is a 409.
73	    """
74	    _validate_provider_env(provider, env)
75	    # Proves the suite exists (404) and the caller may automate it (403).
76	    require_permission(session, suite_id, user_id, minimum="edit")
77	
78	    binding = TriggerBinding(
79	        provider=provider,
80	        pipeline_or_dag_id=pipeline_or_dag_id,
81	        env=env,
82	        suite_id=suite_id,
83	        enabled=enabled,
84	    )
85	    session.add(binding)
86	    try:
87	        session.commit()
88	    except IntegrityError as exc:
89	        session.rollback()
90	        raise TriggerBindingConflictError(
91	            "a binding for this (provider, pipeline, env, suite) already exists",
92	            detail={"provider": provider, "pipeline_or_dag_id": pipeline_or_dag_id, "env": env},
93	        ) from exc
94	    session.refresh(binding)
95	    log.info(
96	        "trigger_binding_created",
97	        binding_id=str(binding.id),
98	        provider=provider,
99	        pipeline_or_dag_id=pipeline_or_dag_id,
100	        env=env,
101	        suite_id=str(suite_id),
102	    )
103	    return binding
104	
105	
106	def list_bindings(
107	    session: Session,
108	    *,
109	    user_id: uuid.UUID,
110	    provider: str | None = None,
111	    env: str | None = None,
112	    suite_id: uuid.UUID | None = None,
113	) -> list[TriggerBinding]:
114	    """Bindings on suites the user can access (owned or shared), newest first."""
115	    accessible = select(Suite.id).where(
116	        or_(
117	            Suite.created_by == user_id,
118	            Suite.id.in_(select(Share.suite_id).where(Share.user_id == user_id)),
119	        )
120	    )
121	    stmt = (
122	        select(TriggerBinding)
123	        .where(TriggerBinding.suite_id.in_(accessible))
124	        .order_by(TriggerBinding.created_at.desc())
125	    )
126	    if provider is not None:
127	        stmt = stmt.where(TriggerBinding.provider == provider)
128	    if env is not None:
129	        stmt = stmt.where(TriggerBinding.env == env)
130	    if suite_id is not None:
131	        stmt = stmt.where(TriggerBinding.suite_id == suite_id)
132	    return list(session.scalars(stmt))
133	
134	
135	def _get_owned(
136	    session: Session, binding_id: uuid.UUID, user_id: uuid.UUID, *, minimum: str
137	) -> TriggerBinding:
138	    """Load a binding and assert the caller's permission on its suite."""
139	    binding = session.get(TriggerBinding, binding_id)
140	    if binding is None:
141	        raise TriggerBindingNotFoundError(
142	            "trigger binding not found", detail={"binding_id": str(binding_id)}
143	        )
144	    require_permission(session, binding.suite_id, user_id, minimum=minimum)
145	    return binding
146	
147	
148	def get_binding(session: Session, binding_id: uuid.UUID, *, user_id: uuid.UUID) -> TriggerBinding:
149	    return _get_owned(session, binding_id, user_id, minimum="view")
150	
151	
152	def update_binding(
153	    session: Session, binding_id: uuid.UUID, *, user_id: uuid.UUID, enabled: bool
154	) -> TriggerBinding:
155	    """Toggle a binding's `enabled` flag. Identity fields are immutable — to
156	    re-target a binding, delete it and create a new one. Requires `edit`."""
157	    binding = _get_owned(session, binding_id, user_id, minimum="edit")
158	    binding.enabled = enabled
159	    session.commit()
160	    session.refresh(binding)
161	    log.info("trigger_binding_updated", binding_id=str(binding.id), enabled=enabled)
162	    return binding
163	
164	
165	def delete_binding(session: Session, binding_id: uuid.UUID, *, user_id: uuid.UUID) -> None:
166	    """Delete a binding. Requires `edit` on its suite."""
167	    binding = _get_owned(session, binding_id, user_id, minimum="edit")
168	    session.delete(binding)
169	    session.commit()
170	    log.info("trigger_binding_deleted", binding_id=str(binding_id))
171	
```

1	"""Celery tasks for asynchronous suite execution.
2	
3	``run_suite`` is the worker entry point dispatched by the manual-run / probe /
4	pipeline-trigger paths. It loads the run's suite / connection / checks, resolves
5	the suite's datasource-shaped **target** (#215) to the runner's
6	``(table, schema, catalog)``, builds the datasource adapter, and hands off to
7	``run_service.execute_run``. The DB-touching core is factored into ``_run_suite``
8	so it can be unit-tested with a fake session + fake runner (no Postgres, no
9	Snowflake); real-DB integration coverage is a Week 8 item.
10	
11	The target lives on the suite (``Suite.target``, resolved by
12	``run_target.resolve_target``): a targetless suite drives the run to ``failed``
13	with a clear log rather than running against an unknown table.
14	"""
15	
16	from __future__ import annotations
17	
18	import uuid
19	from datetime import UTC, datetime, timedelta
20	
21	from sqlalchemy import select
22	from sqlalchemy.orm import Session
23	
24	from backend.app.core.logging import get_logger
25	from backend.app.core.secrets import SecretStore, get_secret_store
26	from backend.app.datasources.flatfile import BatchNotFoundError
27	from backend.app.datasources.registry import build_check_runner
28	from backend.app.db.models import ORCHESTRATION_PROVIDERS, Check, Connection, Run, Suite
29	from backend.app.db.session import get_session
30	from backend.app.orchestration.registry import get_orchestration_provider
31	from backend.app.services import orchestration_service, run_service, run_target
32	from backend.app.worker.celery_app import celery_app
33	
34	# Polling fallback (#171): look back slightly further than the 10-min beat
35	# interval so a run can't slip through the gap between consecutive polls.
36	_POLL_LOOKBACK = timedelta(minutes=15)
37	# Gap recovery (B2): a wider window swept on startup + every 30 min to re-ingest
38	# runs missed while the system was down (worker/beat restart, webhook + poll both
39	# offline). Same provider-agnostic pipeline; only the lookback differs. Safe to
40	# overlap the regular poll — the upsert is idempotent and `skip_updated_since`
41	# drops runs already recorded inside the window.
42	_GAP_RECOVERY_LOOKBACK = timedelta(hours=1)
43	
44	log = get_logger(__name__)
45	
46	
47	def _terminal_failed(session: Session, run: Run, *, event: str, run_id: uuid.UUID) -> str:
48	    """Drive ``run`` to terminal ``failed`` (never left ``queued``/``running``)."""
49	    run.status = "failed"
50	    run.started_at = run.started_at or datetime.now(UTC)
51	    run.finished_at = datetime.now(UTC)
52	    session.commit()
53	    log.exception(event, run_id=str(run_id))
54	    return "failed"
55	
56	
57	def _run_suite(session: Session, *, run_id: uuid.UUID) -> str:
58	    """Load the run's graph, resolve its target, build the runner, execute.
59	
60	    The suite's datasource-shaped ``target`` (#215) resolves to the runner's
```

```
120	    run_service.execute_run(
121	        session, run=run, checks=checks, runner=runner, table=table, schema=target.schema
122	    )
123	    return str(run.status)
124	
125	
126	@celery_app.task(name="run_suite")  # type: ignore[untyped-decorator]  # celery task decorator is unannotated
127	def run_suite(run_id: str) -> str:
128	    """Worker entry point. ``run_id`` is a string so it serialises over JSON.
129	
130	    The target is resolved from the suite (``Suite.target``), so the only
131	    argument the dispatcher supplies is the run id.
132	    """
133	    session = get_session()
134	    try:
135	        return _run_suite(session, run_id=uuid.UUID(run_id))
136	    finally:
137	        session.close()
138	
139	
140	def _poll_orchestration_runs(
141	    session: Session,
142	    *,
143	    secret_store: SecretStore,
144	    now: datetime | None = None,
145	    lookback: timedelta = _POLL_LOOKBACK,
146	) -> dict[str, int]:
147	    """Poll every orchestrator connection for recent succeeded runs (#171, ADR 0004).
148	
149	    The polling fallback for runs that never produced a webhook: iterate each
150	    ADF / Airflow connection, ask the provider's `list_recent_runs` for runs
151	    updated within the ``lookback`` window, and hand them to `ingest_polled_runs`
152	    (upsert + trigger-on-success). Goes through the `OrchestrationProvider` seam —
153	    no per-provider branching. Each connection is isolated: a transport/auth
154	    failure logs + continues so one bad connection can't starve the rest.
155	
156	    ``lookback`` widens for gap recovery (B2): the same sweep over a 1-hour window
157	    re-ingests runs missed during downtime. ``skip_updated_since`` rides the same
158	    window, so a run we already recorded inside it is skipped while a genuinely
159	    missed one (no row) is upserted.
160	    """
161	    since = (now or datetime.now(UTC)) - lookback
162	    summary = {"connections": 0, "recorded": 0, "triggered": 0, "skipped": 0, "errors": 0}
163	    connections = list(
164	        session.scalars(
165	            select(Connection).where(
166	                Connection.type.in_(ORCHESTRATION_PROVIDERS),
167	                Connection.secret_ref.isnot(None),
168	            )
169	        )
170	    )
171	    for connection in connections:
172	        if not connection.secret_ref:  # defensive; the query already filters
173	            continue
174	        try:
175	            provider_impl = get_orchestration_provider(connection.type)
176	            secret = secret_store.get(connection.secret_ref)
177	            updates = provider_impl.list_recent_runs(dict(connection.config), secret, since)
178	            result = orchestration_service.ingest_polled_runs(
179	                session,
180	                provider_impl=provider_impl,
181	                connection=connection,
182	                updates=updates,
183	                skip_updated_since=since,
184	            )
185	            summary["connections"] += 1
186	            summary["recorded"] += len(result.pipeline_runs)
187	            summary["triggered"] += len(result.triggered_runs)
188	            summary["skipped"] += result.skipped
189	        except Exception:
190	            summary["errors"] += 1
191	            session.rollback()
192	            log.exception(
193	                "orchestration_poll_failed",
194	                connection_id=str(connection.id),
195	                provider=connection.type,
196	            )
197	    log.info("orchestration_poll_completed", **summary)
198	    return summary
199	
200	
201	def _run_orchestration_poll(lookback: timedelta) -> dict[str, int]:
202	    """Open a session, run the poll core over ``lookback``, always close.
203	
204	    Shared by both beat entry points (regular poll + gap recovery) so the session
205	    lifecycle lives in one place — the only thing that varies is the window.
206	    """
207	    session = get_session()
208	    try:
209	        return _poll_orchestration_runs(session, secret_store=get_secret_store(), lookback=lookback)
210	    finally:
211	        session.close()
212	
213	
214	@celery_app.task(name="poll_orchestration_runs")  # type: ignore[untyped-decorator]  # celery task decorator is unannotated
215	def poll_orchestration_runs() -> dict[str, int]:
216	    """Celery-beat entry point — the 10-min orchestration polling fallback."""
217	    return _run_orchestration_poll(_POLL_LOOKBACK)
218	
219	
220	@celery_app.task(name="recover_orchestration_gaps")  # type: ignore[untyped-decorator]  # celery task decorator is unannotated
221	def recover_orchestration_gaps() -> dict[str, int]:
222	    """Celery-beat entry point — gap recovery (B2), startup + every 30 min.
223	
224	    The same poll pipeline over the wider ``_GAP_RECOVERY_LOOKBACK`` window, to
225	    re-ingest runs missed while the system was down. Idempotent with the regular
226	    poll (upsert + `skip_updated_since`).
227	    """
228	    return _run_orchestration_poll(_GAP_RECOVERY_LOOKBACK)
229	
```

28	# the score aggregate sums their weights over N. The two operational statuses

30	# (distinct from 'fail', a successful evaluation that breached). Operational
31	# statuses carry NO penalty weight and MUST be excluded from the health-score N

> AGENT

I now have a complete picture of the seams. Let me lay out the plan for **A7 — scheduling backend**.

## A7 plan — DB-backed cron schedules → beat dispatcher

**Model** `schedules` (new table, additive migration off head `e6f7a8b9c0d1`):
- `suite_id` FK (CASCADE), `cron` (5-field), `timezone` (default `UTC`), `enabled`, `next_run_at` (precomputed, indexed), `last_run_at`, `created_by`, timestamps.
- The dispatcher does a cheap `WHERE enabled AND next_run_at <= now()` indexed scan — no per-tick cron parse.

**Cron util** — add `croniter` (runtime dep → `requirements.txt`); validate cron + IANA timezone, compute next fire. **No-backfill semantics**: on fire, advance `next_run_at` to the next *future* occurrence, so a downtime gap fires once, not N times (correct for monitoring).

**Service** `schedule_service` — CRUD mirroring `trigger_binding_service` (suite-permission-gated: `edit` to mutate, `view` to read). Cron/tz validated at write; suite target validated at *fire* time (schedule before configuring target is allowed).

**API** `schedules.py` — POST/GET/GET{id}/PATCH/DELETE, mounted in the v1 router.

**Beat task** `dispatch_due_schedules` (60s beat entry) — selects due+enabled schedules, resolves target up front (skip+log invalid, but still advance to avoid hot-loop), creates `Run(triggered_by="schedule:{id}")`, advances `next_run_at`, dispatches via `run_dispatch` (broker-fail → `mark_dispatch_failed`). Reuses the exact run-creation contract from `_trigger_suites`.

This is an implementation of an existing roadmap milestone (not a new architectural decision), so no new ADR — but I'll note the no-backfill choice in code.

Let me confirm two design forks before I build, since they shape the contract:

{
  "questions": [
    {
      "question": "When the worker/beat is down across one or more scheduled fire times, how should a schedule behave on recovery?",
      "header": "Catch-up",
      "multiSelect": false,
      "options": [
        {
          "label": "Fire once, no backfill (Recommended)",
          "description": "On recovery, advance next_run_at to the next future occurrence and fire at most once. Standard monitoring semantics — avoids a thundering herd of backfill runs after a deploy/outage."
        },
        {
          "label": "Backfill every missed slot",
          "description": "Queue one run per missed interval. Truer to 'ran every hour' but can stampede the worker after long downtime and produce noisy duplicate alerts."
        }
      ]
    },
    {
      "question": "Should a schedule support an arbitrary IANA timezone, or UTC-only for v1?",
      "header": "Timezone",
      "multiSelect": false,
      "options": [
        {
          "label": "IANA tz, default UTC (Recommended)",
          "description": "Store a validated timezone string (e.g. America/New_York); compute next_run_at DST-aware via zoneinfo. Matters for 'run at 6am ET after the nightly load'. Slightly more code + tests."
        },
        {
          "label": "UTC-only for v1",
          "description": "Cron always evaluated in UTC. Simpler, but users must hand-convert business hours and it breaks across DST."
        }
      ]
    }
  ]
}

  "new_string": "class Schedule(Base):\n    \"\"\"A cron schedule that fires a suite run automatically (A7).\n\n    The beat dispatcher (`worker.tasks.dispatch_due_schedules`) ticks every\n    minute and queries `enabled AND next_run_at <= now()` — an indexed scan, so\n    the cron is parsed only when a schedule actually fires, never per tick.\n\n    `next_run_at` is precomputed (`services.cron.next_fire`) on create and after\n    each fire / cron change. **No-backfill semantics**: a fire advances it to the\n    next *future* occurrence, so a downtime gap fires at most once on recovery\n    rather than backfilling every missed slot (correct for monitoring). `cron` is\n    a standard 5-field expression evaluated in `timezone` (IANA, DST-aware,\n    default UTC). Cascade-deleted with the suite.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    __tablename__ = \"schedules\"\n    __table_args__ = (\n        Index(\"ix_schedules_suite_id\", \"suite_id\"),\n        # The dispatcher's hot path: due + enabled schedules, oldest-due first.\n        Index(\"ix_schedules_enabled_next_run_at\", \"enabled\", \"next_run_at\"),\n    )\n\n    id: Mapped[uuid.UUID] = _uuid_pk()\n    suite_id: Mapped[uuid.UUID] = mapped_column(\n        UUID(as_uuid=True), ForeignKey(\"suites.id\", ondelete=\"CASCADE\"), nullable=False\n    )\n    cron: Mapped[str] = mapped_column(String(128), nullable=False)\n    # IANA tz name the cron is evaluated in (e.g. 'America/New_York'); 'UTC' default.\n    timezone: Mapped[str] = mapped_column(\n        String(64), nullable=False, server_default=text(\"'UTC'\")\n    )\n    enabled: Mapped[bool] = mapped_column(Boolean, nullable=False, server_default=text(\"true\"))\n    # Precomputed next fire (UTC). The dispatcher scans on this; never parses cron.\n    next_run_at: Mapped[datetime] = mapped_column(DateTime(timezone=True), nullable=False)\n    # Last time the dispatcher fired this schedule (NULL until first fire).\n    last_run_at: Mapped[datetime | None] = mapped_column(DateTime(timezone=True))\n    created_by: Mapped[uuid.UUID] = mapped_column(\n        UUID(as_uuid=True), ForeignKey(\"users.id\"), nullable=False\n    )\n    created_at: Mapped[datetime] = _created_at()\n    updated_at: Mapped[datetime] = _updated_at()\n\n\n__all__ = [\n    \"Base\",\n    \"Check\",\n    \"Connection\",\n    \"PipelineRun\",\n    \"Result\",\n    \"Run\",\n    \"Schedule\",\n    \"Share\",\n    \"Suite\",\n    \"TriggerBinding\",\n    \"User\",\n]"

{
  "file_path": "/Users/arijit/Coding/Python/DataQ/backend/app/services/cron.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"Cron-expression helpers for suite run schedules (A7).\n\nThin, FastAPI-free wrapper over `croniter` + `zoneinfo`: validate a 5-field cron\nexpression and an IANA timezone, and compute the next fire time. The schedule\nmodel stores `next_run_at` precomputed by `next_fire` so the beat dispatcher\nnever parses cron on its hot path — see `db.models.Schedule`.\n\n**No-backfill semantics** live here: `next_fire` always returns the next fire\n*strictly after* its base instant, so advancing a fired schedule jumps past any\nslots missed during downtime — a recovery fires at most once, not once per\nmissed interval (correct for monitoring; confirmed design choice).\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nfrom datetime import UTC, datetime\nfrom zoneinfo import ZoneInfo, ZoneInfoNotFoundError\n\nfrom croniter import croniter\n\nfrom backend.app.core.errors import DataQError\n\n\nclass InvalidCronError(DataQError):\n    status_code = 422\n    code = \"invalid_cron\"\n\n\nclass InvalidTimezoneError(DataQError):\n    status_code = 422\n    code = \"invalid_timezone\"\n\n\ndef validate_timezone(timezone: str) -> ZoneInfo:\n    \"\"\"Resolve an IANA timezone name or raise a 422. Returns the `ZoneInfo`.\"\"\"\n    try:\n        return ZoneInfo(timezone)\n    except (ZoneInfoNotFoundError, ValueError) as exc:\n        raise InvalidTimezoneError(\n            f\"unknown timezone {timezone!r}\", detail={\"timezone\": timezone}\n        ) from exc\n\n\ndef validate_cron(expression: str) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Validate a 5-field cron expression or raise a 422.\n\n    `croniter.is_valid` also accepts its non-standard extensions (e.g. `@hourly`,\n    seconds fields); we keep to the documented 5-field standard so the stored\n    expression is portable and the UI can render it predictably.\n    \"\"\"\n    fields = expression.split()\n    if len(fields) != 5:\n        raise InvalidCronError(\n            \"cron expression must have exactly 5 fields (min hour dom mon dow)\",\n            detail={\"cron\": expression},\n        )\n    if not croniter.is_valid(expression):\n        raise InvalidCronError(f\"invalid cron expression {expression!r}\", detail={\"cron\": expression})\n\n\ndef next_fire(expression: str, timezone: str, *, after: datetime | None = None) -> datetime:\n    \"\"\"Next fire time strictly after ``after`` (default now), as a UTC-aware datetime.\n\n    The cron is evaluated in ``timezone`` (DST-aware), so \"0 6 * * *\" in\n    America/New_York fires at 06:00 local across DST boundaries; the returned\n    instant is normalised to UTC for storage on ``Schedule.next_run_at``.\n    Validates both inputs first (422 on either).\n    \"\"\"\n    validate_cron(expression)\n    tz = validate_timezone(timezone)\n    base = (after or datetime.now(UTC)).astimezone(tz)\n    nxt: datetime = croniter(expression, base).get_next(datetime)\n    return nxt.astimezone(UTC)\n"
}

7	Connections carried no history — ``connection_service.update_connection``
8	overwrote the row in place, so a prior name/config was unrecoverable. This adds a
9	``connection_versions`` table holding an immutable snapshot of a connection's
10	editable, **non-secret** state, written on create and after every successful
11	name/config update; it backs the connection "version history" view. Mirrors
12	``check_versions`` — per-connection config history, not the cross-entity audit
13	log (deferred, #310).
14

16	SecretStore (referenced by the constant ``conn-<id>`` pointer), so it is never
17	copied here — a credential rotation records no version.

19	``version_no`` is a per-connection sequence (unique with ``connection_id``). A

```json
{
  "file_path": "/Users/arijit/Coding/Python/DataQ/backend/app/services/schedule_service.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"Schedule CRUD — cron-driven suite run schedules (A7).\n\nA `schedule` fires a suite run on a cron cadence: (`cron`, `timezone`) → `suite_id`.\nThe beat dispatcher (`worker.tasks.dispatch_due_schedules`) *consumes* enabled,\ndue schedules; this module lets users *manage* them. Mirrors\n`trigger_binding_service`: FastAPI-free (takes a `Session`, returns ORM models,\nraises typed `DataQError`s), and gated on the caller's suite permission\n(`edit` to create / change / delete, `view` to read) so you can't schedule a\nsuite you can't access.\n\n`next_run_at` is kept consistent here: computed on create, and recomputed by\n`update_schedule` whenever the cron / timezone changes or a paused schedule is\nre-enabled (so re-enabling never fires an immediate backlog — `services.cron`\nreturns the next *future* fire). Pausing leaves `next_run_at` as-is; the\ndispatcher's `enabled` filter excludes it regardless.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport uuid\n\nfrom sqlalchemy import or_, select\nfrom sqlalchemy.orm import Session\n\nfrom backend.app.core.logging import get_logger\nfrom backend.app.db.models import Schedule, Share, Suite\nfrom backend.app.services import cron\nfrom backend.app.services.suite_authz import require_permission\n\nlog = get_logger(__name__)\n\n\ndef create_schedule(\n    session: Session,\n    *,\n    suite_id: uuid.UUID,\n    cron_expr: str,\n    user_id: uuid.UUID,\n    timezone: str = \"UTC\",\n    enabled: bool = True,\n) -> Schedule:\n    \"\"\"Create a schedule. Requires `edit` on the target suite (404/403 otherwise).\n\n    The cron / timezone are validated (422 on either) and the first `next_run_at`\n    is computed before insert. The suite's run *target* is not required here — a\n    suite can be scheduled before its target is configured; the dispatcher\n    re-checks the target at fire time and skips (with a log) if still invalid.\n    \"\"\"\n    # Proves the suite exists (404) and the caller may automate it (403).\n    require_permission(session, suite_id, user_id, minimum=\"edit\")\n    next_run_at = cron.next_fire(cron_expr, timezone)  # validates cron + tz (422)\n\n    schedule = Schedule(\n        suite_id=suite_id,\n        cron=cron_expr,\n        timezone=timezone,\n        enabled=enabled,\n        next_run_at=next_run_at,\n    )\n    schedule.created_by = user_id\n    session.add(schedule)\n    session.commit()\n    session.refresh(schedule)\n    log.info(\n        \"schedule_created\",\n        schedule_id=str(schedule.id),\n        suite_id=str(suite_id),\n        cron=cron_expr,\n        timezone=timezone,\n        enabled=enabled,\n    )\n    return schedule\n\n\ndef list_schedules(\n    session: Session,\n    *,\n    user_id: uuid.UUID,\n    suite_id: uuid.UUID | None = None,\n    enabled: bool | None = None,\n) -> list[Schedule]:\n    \"\"\"Schedules on suites the user can access (owned or shared), newest first.\"\"\"\n    accessible = select(Suite.id).where(\n        or_(\n            Suite.created_by == user_id,\n            Suite.id.in_(select(Share.suite_id).where(Share.user_id == user_id)),\n        )\n    )\n    stmt = (\n        select(Schedule)\n        .where(Schedule.suite_id.in_(accessible))\n        .order_by(Schedule.created_at.desc())\n    )\n    if suite_id is not None:\n        stmt = stmt.where(Schedule.suite_id == suite_id)\n    if enabled is not None:\n        stmt = stmt.where(Schedule.enabled.is_(enabled))\n    return list(session.scalars(stmt))\n\n\ndef _get_owned(\n    session: Session, schedule_id: uuid.UUID, user_id: uuid.UUID, *, minimum: str\n) -> Schedule:\n    \"\"\"Load a schedule and assert the caller's permission on its suite.\"\"\"\n    schedule = session.get(Schedule, schedule_id)\n    if schedule is None:\n        raise ScheduleNotFoundError(\n            \"schedule not found\", detail={\"schedule_id\": str(schedule_id)}\n        )\n    require_permission(session, schedule.suite_id, user_id, minimum=minimum)\n    return schedule\n\n\ndef get_schedule(session: Session, schedule_id: uuid.UUID, *, user_id: uuid.UUID) -> Schedule:\n    return _get_owned(session, schedule_id, user_id, minimum=\"view\")\n\n\ndef update_schedule(\n    session: Session,\n    schedule_id: uuid.UUID,\n    *,\n    user_id: uuid.UUID,\n    cron_expr: str | None = None,\n    timezone: str | None = None,\n    enabled: bool | None = None,\n) -> Schedule:\n    \"\"\"Patch a schedule's cron / timezone / enabled flag. Requires `edit`.\n\n    `next_run_at` is recomputed when the cadence changes (cron or timezone) and\n    when a paused schedule is re-enabled, so re-enabling resumes from the next\n    future fire rather than firing every slot missed while paused.\n    \"\"\"\n    schedule = _get_owned(session, schedule_id, user_id, minimum=\"edit\")\n\n    new_cron = cron_expr if cron_expr is not None else schedule.cron\n    new_tz = timezone if timezone is not None else schedule.timezone\n    cadence_changed = (cron_expr is not None and cron_expr != schedule.cron) or (\n        timezone is not None and timezone != schedule.timezone\n    )\n    re_enabling = enabled is True and not schedule.enabled\n\n    if cron_expr is not None:\n        schedule.cron = new_cron\n    if timezone is not None:\n        schedule.timezone = new_tz\n    if enabled is not None:\n        schedule.enabled = enabled\n    if cadence_changed or re_enabling:\n        # Validates the (possibly new) cron + tz (422) and re-bases the fire time.\n        schedule.next_run_at = cron.next_fire(new_cron, new_tz)\n\n    session.commit()\n    session.refresh(schedule)\n    log.info(\n        \"schedule_updated\",\n        schedule_id=str(schedule.id),\n        cron=schedule.cron,\n        timezone=schedule.timezone,\n        enabled=schedule.enabled,\n    )\n    return schedule\n\n\ndef delete_schedule(session: Session, schedule_id: uuid.UUID, *, user_id: uuid.UUID) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Delete a schedule. Requires `edit` on its suite.\"\"\"\n    schedule = _get_owned(session, schedule_id, user_id, minimum=\"edit\")\n    session.delete(schedule)\n    session.commit()\n    log.info(\"schedule_deleted\", schedule_id=str(schedule_id))\n\n\nfrom backend.app.core.errors import DataQError  # noqa: E402  (kept near its use; mirrors module style)\n\n\nclass ScheduleNotFoundError(DataQError):\n    status_code = 404\n    code = \"schedule_not_found\"\n"
}

  "content": "\"\"\"Schedule CRUD endpoints — manage cron-driven suite run schedules (A7).\n\nThin HTTP layer over `schedule_service`: a schedule fires a suite run on a cron\ncadence (`cron` + `timezone` → `suite_id`). All validation (cron / timezone /\nsuite-permission) and `next_run_at` bookkeeping live in the service.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport uuid\nfrom datetime import datetime\nfrom typing import Annotated\n\nfrom fastapi import APIRouter, Depends, status\nfrom pydantic import BaseModel, ConfigDict, Field\nfrom sqlalchemy.orm import Session\n\nfrom backend.app.core.auth import get_current_user\nfrom backend.app.db.models import Schedule, User\nfrom backend.app.db.session import get_db\nfrom backend.app.services import schedule_service as svc\n\nrouter = APIRouter(tags=[\"schedules\"])\n\n\nclass ScheduleCreate(BaseModel):\n    suite_id: uuid.UUID\n    cron: str = Field(min_length=1, max_length=128)\n    timezone: str = Field(default=\"UTC\", min_length=1, max_length=64)\n    enabled: bool = True\n\n\nclass ScheduleUpdate(BaseModel):\n    \"\"\"Partial update — only the supplied fields change. `next_run_at` is\n    recomputed by the service when the cadence changes or a paused schedule is\n    re-enabled.\"\"\"\n\n    cron: str | None = Field(default=None, min_length=1, max_length=128)\n    timezone: str | None = Field(default=None, min_length=1, max_length=64)\n    enabled: bool | None = None\n\n\nclass ScheduleRead(BaseModel):\n    model_config = ConfigDict(from_attributes=True)\n\n    id: uuid.UUID\n    suite_id: uuid.UUID\n    cron: str\n    timezone: str\n    enabled: bool\n    next_run_at: datetime\n    last_run_at: datetime | None\n\n\n@router.post(\n    \"/schedules\",\n    response_model=ScheduleRead,\n    status_code=status.HTTP_201_CREATED,\n    summary=\"Create a suite run schedule\",\n)\ndef create_schedule(\n    payload: ScheduleCreate,\n    current_user: Annotated[User, Depends(get_current_user)],\n    db: Annotated[Session, Depends(get_db)],\n) -> Schedule:\n    return svc.create_schedule(\n        db,\n        suite_id=payload.suite_id,\n        cron_expr=payload.cron,\n        timezone=payload.timezone,\n        enabled=payload.enabled,\n        user_id=current_user.id,\n    )\n\n\n@router.get(\n    \"/schedules\",\n    response_model=list[ScheduleRead],\n    summary=\"List schedules on accessible suites\",\n)\ndef list_schedules(\n    current_user: Annotated[User, Depends(get_current_user)],\n    db: Annotated[Session, Depends(get_db)],\n    suite_id: uuid.UUID | None = None,\n    enabled: bool | None = None,\n) -> list[Schedule]:\n    return svc.list_schedules(\n        db, user_id=current_user.id, suite_id=suite_id, enabled=enabled\n    )\n\n\n@router.get(\n    \"/schedules/{schedule_id}\",\n    response_model=ScheduleRead,\n    summary=\"Get a schedule\",\n)\ndef get_schedule(\n    schedule_id: uuid.UUID,\n    current_user: Annotated[User, Depends(get_current_user)],\n    db: Annotated[Session, Depends(get_db)],\n) -> Schedule:\n    return svc.get_schedule(db, schedule_id, user_id=current_user.id)\n\n\n@router.patch(\n    \"/schedules/{schedule_id}\",\n    response_model=ScheduleRead,\n    summary=\"Update a schedule (cron / timezone / enabled)\",\n)\ndef update_schedule(\n    schedule_id: uuid.UUID,\n    payload: ScheduleUpdate,\n    current_user: Annotated[User, Depends(get_current_user)],\n    db: Annotated[Session, Depends(get_db)],\n) -> Schedule:\n    return svc.update_schedule(\n        db,\n        schedule_id,\n        user_id=current_user.id,\n        cron_expr=payload.cron,\n        timezone=payload.timezone,\n        enabled=payload.enabled,\n    )\n\n\n@router.delete(\n    \"/schedules/{schedule_id}\",\n    status_code=status.HTTP_204_NO_CONTENT,\n    summary=\"Delete a schedule\",\n)\ndef delete_schedule(\n    schedule_id: uuid.UUID,\n    current_user: Annotated[User, Depends(get_current_user)],\n    db: Annotated[Session, Depends(get_db)],\n) -> None:\n    svc.delete_schedule(db, schedule_id, user_id=current_user.id)\n"
}

```
82	def resolve_target(conn_type: str, target: dict[str, Any] | None) -> ResolvedTarget:
83	    """Resolve ``target`` for a ``conn_type`` connection, or raise (422).
84	
85	    Raises `SuiteTargetInvalidError` if the suite is targetless, the target is
86	    missing the field its datasource requires (`path` for flat files, `table`
87	    for SQL, `catalog` for Unity Catalog), or the connection type has no run path
88	    (orchestration providers — they are never suite datasources).
89	    """
90	    if not target:
91	        raise SuiteTargetInvalidError(
92	            "suite has no target configured", detail={"connection_type": conn_type}
93	        )
94	
95	    if conn_type in _FLATFILE_TYPES:
96	        # A batch target (regex `pattern`) is resolved to a concrete path at run
97	        # time; a literal target carries the `path` directly. The two are mutually
98	        # exclusive — both set is an ambiguous target, not a silent batch win.
99	        if "pattern" in target and target.get("path"):
100	            raise SuiteTargetInvalidError(
101	                "flat-file target is ambiguous: set either 'path' (literal) or "
102	                "'pattern' (batch), not both",
103	                detail={"connection_type": conn_type},
104	            )
105	        if "pattern" in target:
106	            return ResolvedTarget(
107	                table="", schema=None, catalog=None, batch=_batch_spec(target, conn_type)
108	            )
109	        path = _require(target, "path", conn_type)
110	        return ResolvedTarget(table=path, schema=None, catalog=None)
111	
```

  "new_string": "    The same poll pipeline over the wider ``_GAP_RECOVERY_LOOKBACK`` window, to\n    re-ingest runs missed while the system was down. Idempotent with the regular\n    poll (upsert + `skip_updated_since`).\n    \"\"\"\n    return _run_orchestration_poll(_GAP_RECOVERY_LOOKBACK)\n\n\n# ──────────────────────── scheduled run dispatch (A7) ──────────────────────\n\n\ndef _advance_schedule(session: Session, schedule: Schedule, *, now: datetime) -> bool:\n    \"\"\"Roll ``schedule`` forward to its next future fire and stamp ``last_run_at``.\n\n    **No-backfill semantics**: ``cron.next_fire`` returns the next occurrence\n    strictly after ``now``, so a gap (worker/beat down across several slots) is\n    collapsed to a single fire rather than backfilled. Returns True if advanced;\n    False (and disables the schedule) if the stored cron/tz is somehow invalid —\n    validated on write, so this only guards against direct DB tampering and stops\n    an un-advanceable row from hot-looping the dispatcher every tick.\n    \"\"\"\n    schedule.last_run_at = now\n    try:\n        schedule.next_run_at = cron.next_fire(schedule.cron, schedule.timezone, after=now)\n    except DataQError:\n        schedule.enabled = False\n        log.error(\n            \"schedule_disabled_invalid_cron\",\n            schedule_id=str(schedule.id),\n            cron=schedule.cron,\n            timezone=schedule.timezone,\n        )\n        return False\n    return True\n\n\ndef _fire_schedule(session: Session, schedule: Schedule, *, now: datetime) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Fire one due schedule: advance it, then queue + dispatch a suite run.\n\n    Advancing ``next_run_at`` happens **before** the run is created and is\n    committed in every branch, so the schedule leaves the due window for this\n    tick whatever the run's fate — a misconfigured suite never hot-loops. The run\n    is created with the canonical ``schedule:<id>`` ``triggered_by`` marker and\n    handed to the worker exactly like the manual / pipeline-trigger paths; a\n    targetless suite is skipped (not queued-then-failed), and a broker outage\n    marks the run ``failed`` rather than leaving it stuck ``queued`` (#227).\n    \"\"\"\n    if not _advance_schedule(session, schedule, now=now):\n        session.commit()\n        return \"disabled\"\n\n    suite = session.get(Suite, schedule.suite_id)\n    assert suite is not None  # schedule cascade-deletes with its suite\n    connection = session.get(Connection, suite.connection_id)\n    assert connection is not None  # suite.connection_id FK is RESTRICT\n    try:\n        run_target.resolve_target(connection.type, suite.target)\n    except DataQError:\n        session.commit()  # persist the advance; skip the doomed run\n        log.warning(\n            \"schedule_skipped_invalid_target\",\n            schedule_id=str(schedule.id),\n            suite_id=str(suite.id),\n        )\n        return \"skipped_target\"\n\n    run = Run(suite_id=suite.id, status=\"queued\", triggered_by=f\"schedule:{schedule.id}\")\n    session.add(run)\n    session.commit()\n    session.refresh(run)\n    try:\n        run.celery_task_id = run_dispatch.dispatch_run(run.id)\n        session.commit()\n    except Exception:\n        run_dispatch.mark_dispatch_failed(run)\n        session.commit()\n        log.exception(\"schedule_dispatch_failed\", schedule_id=str(schedule.id), run_id=str(run.id))\n        return \"dispatch_failed\"\n    log.info(\"schedule_fired\", schedule_id=str(schedule.id), run_id=str(run.id))\n    return \"dispatched\"\n\n\ndef _dispatch_due_schedules(session: Session, *, now: datetime | None = None) -> dict[str, int]:\n    \"\"\"Fire every enabled schedule whose ``next_run_at`` has passed (A7).\n\n    Pulls due schedules one at a time with ``FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`` so two\n    overlapping dispatcher ticks can't double-fire the same schedule: the second\n    skips a row the first holds, and once fired the row's ``next_run_at`` is past\n    ``now`` so it drops out of the due set. ``now`` is fixed at entry, so the loop\n    is finite (each iteration advances one row out of the window).\n    \"\"\"\n    now = now or datetime.now(UTC)\n    summary = {\"due\": 0, \"dispatched\": 0, \"skipped_target\": 0, \"dispatch_failed\": 0, \"disabled\": 0}\n    while True:\n        schedule = session.scalars(\n            select(Schedule)\n            .where(Schedule.enabled.is_(True), Schedule.next_run_at <= now)\n            .order_by(Schedule.next_run_at)\n            .with_for_update(skip_locked=True)\n            .limit(1)\n        ).first()\n        if schedule is None:\n            break\n        summary[\"due\"] += 1\n        outcome = _fire_schedule(session, schedule, now=now)\n        summary[outcome] = summary.get(outcome, 0) + 1\n    log.info(\"schedules_dispatch_completed\", **summary)\n    return summary\n\n\n@celery_app.task(name=\"dispatch_due_schedules\")  # type: ignore[untyped-decorator]  # celery task decorator is unannotated\ndef dispatch_due_schedules() -> dict[str, int]:\n    \"\"\"Celery-beat entry point — fire due suite-run schedules (A7), every minute.\"\"\"\n    session = get_session()\n    try:\n        return _dispatch_due_schedules(session)\n    finally:\n        session.close()"
}
```

1	"""Trigger-binding endpoint tests (TestClient + real Postgres).
2	
3	get_db is overridden to the shared test session; auth runs in dev-bypass mode
4	(conftest) so the caller is the dev user. Suites created via the API are owned by
5	that user (edit allowed); a directly-inserted suite with a different owner is
6	used to exercise the access-control paths. Skips without TEST_DATABASE_URL.
7	"""
8	
9	import uuid
10	from collections.abc import Iterator
11	from typing import Any
12	
13	import pytest
14	from fastapi.testclient import TestClient
15	from sqlalchemy import func, select
16	
17	from backend.app.db.models import Connection, Suite, TriggerBinding, User
18	from backend.app.db.session import get_db
19	from backend.app.main import app
20	
21	
22	@pytest.fixture
23	def client(db_session: Any) -> Iterator[TestClient]:
24	    app.dependency_overrides[get_db] = lambda: db_session
25	    try:
26	        yield TestClient(app)
27	    finally:
28	        app.dependency_overrides.clear()
29	
30	
31	def _connection(db_session: Any) -> Connection:
32	    owner = User(aad_object_id=uuid.uuid4().hex, email="owner@example.com")
33	    db_session.add(owner)
34	    db_session.flush()
35	    conn = Connection(
36	        name=f"sf-{uuid.uuid4().hex[:8]}",
37	        type="snowflake",
38	        env="dev",
39	        config={"account": "ab12345.eu-west-1"},
40	        secret_ref="kv-sf",
41	        created_by=owner.id,
42	    )
43	    db_session.add(conn)
44	    db_session.commit()
45	    return conn
46	
47	
48	def _owned_suite(client: TestClient, connection_id: uuid.UUID) -> str:
49	    """Create a suite via the API so it's owned by the dev-bypass caller."""
50	    resp = client.post(
51	        "/api/v1/suites",
52	        json={"name": f"s-{uuid.uuid4().hex[:8]}", "connection_id": str(connection_id)},
53	    )
54	    assert resp.status_code == 201, resp.text
55	    return str(resp.json()["id"])
56	
57	
58	def _unowned_suite(db_session: Any, connection: Connection) -> Suite:
59	    """A suite owned by someone else, not shared with the caller → no access."""
60	    other = User(aad_object_id=uuid.uuid4().hex, email="other@example.com")
61	    db_session.add(other)
62	    db_session.flush()
63	    suite = Suite(name="theirs", connection_id=connection.id, created_by=other.id)
64	    db_session.add(suite)
65	    db_session.commit()
66	    return suite
67	
68	
69	def _payload(suite_id: str, **overrides: Any) -> dict[str, Any]:
70	    body: dict[str, Any] = {
71	        "provider": "adf",
72	        "pipeline_or_dag_id": "load_finance",
73	        "env": "dev",
74	        "suite_id": suite_id,
75	    }
76	    body.update(overrides)
77	    return body
78	
79	
80	def test_create_then_get_binding(client: TestClient, db_session: Any) -> None:
81	    suite_id = _owned_suite(client, _connection(db_session).id)
82	    created = client.post("/api/v1/trigger-bindings", json=_payload(suite_id))
83	    assert created.status_code == 201, created.text
84	    body = created.json()
85	    assert body["provider"] == "adf"
86	    assert body["enabled"] is True
87	
88	    got = client.get(f"/api/v1/trigger-bindings/{body['id']}")
89	    assert got.status_code == 200
90	    assert got.json()["suite_id"] == suite_id
91	
92	
93	def test_create_rejects_unknown_provider(client: TestClient, db_session: Any) -> None:
94	    suite_id = _owned_suite(client, _connection(db_session).id)
95	    resp = client.post("/api/v1/trigger-bindings", json=_payload(suite_id, provider="dbt"))
96	    assert resp.status_code == 422
97	    assert resp.json()["error"]["code"] == "trigger_binding_invalid"
98	
99	
100	def test_duplicate_binding_conflicts(client: TestClient, db_session: Any) -> None:
101	    suite_id = _owned_suite(client, _connection(db_session).id)
102	    first = client.post("/api/v1/trigger-bindings", json=_payload(suite_id))
103	    assert first.status_code == 201
104	    dup = client.post("/api/v1/trigger-bindings", json=_payload(suite_id))
105	    assert dup.status_code == 409
106	    assert dup.json()["error"]["code"] == "trigger_binding_conflict"
107	
108	
109	def test_create_on_inaccessible_suite_is_404(client: TestClient, db_session: Any) -> None:
110	    # A suite the caller has no access to is hidden (404), not 403 — and no row.
111	    conn = _connection(db_session)
112	    suite = _unowned_suite(db_session, conn)
113	    resp = client.post("/api/v1/trigger-bindings", json=_payload(str(suite.id)))
114	    assert resp.status_code == 404
115	    assert db_session.scalar(select(func.count()).select_from(TriggerBinding)) == 0
116	
117	
118	def test_create_with_view_only_is_forbidden(client: TestClient, db_session: Any) -> None:
119	    from backend.app.core.auth import DEV_BYPASS_AAD_OID
120	    from backend.app.db.models import Share
121	
122	    conn = _connection(db_session)
123	    suite = _unowned_suite(db_session, conn)
124	    # warm up auth so the dev-bypass user row exists, then share at view-only
125	    client.get("/api/v1/trigger-bindings")
126	    me = db_session.scalar(select(User).where(User.aad_object_id == DEV_BYPASS_AAD_OID))
127	    db_session.add(Share(suite_id=suite.id, user_id=me.id, permission="view"))
128	    db_session.commit()
129	
130	    # creating a binding needs `edit`; view-only → 403 (access exists, too low)
131	    resp = client.post("/api/v1/trigger-bindings", json=_payload(str(suite.id)))
132	    assert resp.status_code == 403
133	
134	
135	def test_create_on_missing_suite_404(client: TestClient) -> None:
136	    resp = client.post("/api/v1/trigger-bindings", json=_payload(str(uuid.uuid4())))
137	    assert resp.status_code == 404
138	
139	
140	def test_list_is_scoped_to_accessible_suites(client: TestClient, db_session: Any) -> None:
141	    conn = _connection(db_session)
142	    mine = _owned_suite(client, conn.id)
143	    client.post("/api/v1/trigger-bindings", json=_payload(mine))
144	    # a binding on a suite I don't own (inserted directly) must not show
145	    theirs = _unowned_suite(db_session, conn)
146	    db_session.add(
147	        TriggerBinding(provider="adf", pipeline_or_dag_id="other", env="dev", suite_id=theirs.id)
148	    )
149	    db_session.commit()
150	
151	    listed = client.get("/api/v1/trigger-bindings")
152	    assert listed.status_code == 200
153	    suite_ids = {b["suite_id"] for b in listed.json()}
154	    assert suite_ids == {mine}
155	
156	
157	def test_toggle_then_delete(client: TestClient, db_session: Any) -> None:
158	    suite_id = _owned_suite(client, _connection(db_session).id)
159	    bid = client.post("/api/v1/trigger-bindings", json=_payload(suite_id)).json()["id"]
160	
161	    disabled = client.patch(f"/api/v1/trigger-bindings/{bid}", json={"enabled": False})
162	    assert disabled.status_code == 200
163	    assert disabled.json()["enabled"] is False
164	
165	    deleted = client.delete(f"/api/v1/trigger-bindings/{bid}")
166	    assert deleted.status_code == 204
167	    assert client.get(f"/api/v1/trigger-bindings/{bid}").status_code == 404
168
