1	"""Tests for the run_suite Celery task orchestration.
2	
3	No Postgres, no GX, no broker: a fake Session serves the run graph from memory,
4	``build_check_runner`` is monkeypatched to a fake CheckRunner, and the task
5	core (`_run_suite`) is called directly. Real-DB integration coverage is a Week 8
6	item (Postgres test fixtures).
7	"""
8	
9	import uuid
10	from typing import Any
11	
12	import pytest
13	
14	from backend.app.datasources.base import CheckOutcome, CheckSpec, SuiteOutcome
15	from backend.app.db.models import Check, Connection, Run, Suite
16	from backend.app.worker import tasks
17	
18	
19	class FakeSession:
20	    def __init__(
21	        self,
22	        *,
23	        run: Run | None = None,
24	        suite: Suite | None = None,
25	        connection: Connection | None = None,
26	        checks: tuple[Check, ...] = (),
27	    ) -> None:
28	        self._objs: dict[type, Any] = {Run: run, Suite: suite, Connection: connection}
29	        self._checks = list(checks)
30	        self.added: list[Any] = []
31	        self.commits = 0
32	        self.rollbacks = 0
33	        self.closed = False
34	
35	    def get(self, model: type, pk: Any) -> Any:
36	        return self._objs.get(model)
37	
38	    def scalars(self, _stmt: Any) -> Any:
39	        return iter(self._checks)
40	
41	    def add_all(self, rows: list[Any]) -> None:
42	        self.added.extend(rows)
43	
44	    def commit(self) -> None:
45	        self.commits += 1
46	
47	    def rollback(self) -> None:
48	        self.rollbacks += 1
49	        self.added.clear()
50	
51	    def refresh(self, obj: object) -> None:  # no-op reload (status unchanged here)
52	        pass
53	
54	    def close(self) -> None:
55	        self.closed = True
56	
57	
58	class FakeRunner:
59	    def __init__(self, outcome: SuiteOutcome) -> None:
60	        self._outcome = outcome
61	        self.table: str | None = None
62	
63	    def run_checks(
64	        self, *, table: str, schema: str | None, checks: list[CheckSpec]
65	    ) -> SuiteOutcome:
66	        self.table = table
67	        return self._outcome
68	
69	
70	def _graph(n_checks: int = 1) -> tuple[Run, Suite, Connection, tuple[Check, ...]]:
71	    suite_id = uuid.uuid4()
72	    conn_id = uuid.uuid4()
73	    user_id = uuid.uuid4()
74	    run = Run(id=uuid.uuid4(), suite_id=suite_id, status="queued")
75	    suite = Suite(
76	        id=suite_id,
77	        name="probe",
78	        connection_id=conn_id,
79	        created_by=user_id,
80	        target={"table": "ORDERS"},
81	    )
82	    connection = Connection(
83	        id=conn_id,
84	        name="probe-sf",
85	        type="snowflake",
86	        env="dev",
87	        config={"account": "a"},
88	        secret_ref="snowflake-dev",
89	        created_by=user_id,
90	    )
91	    checks = tuple(
92	        Check(
93	            id=uuid.uuid4(),
94	            suite_id=suite_id,
95	            name=f"c{i}",
96	            kind="expectation",
97	            expectation_type="x",
98	            config={},
99	        )
100	        for i in range(n_checks)
101	    )
102	    return run, suite, connection, checks
103	
104	
105	# ───────────────────────── happy path ──────────────────────────────
106	
107	
108	def test_run_suite_executes_and_persists(monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch) -> None:
109	    run, suite, connection, checks = _graph(2)
110	    session = FakeSession(run=run, suite=suite, connection=connection, checks=checks)
111	    runner = FakeRunner(
112	        SuiteOutcome(
113	            success=True,
114	            checks=[CheckOutcome("x", success=True), CheckOutcome("x", success=True)],
115	        )
116	    )
117	    monkeypatch.setattr(tasks, "build_check_runner", lambda **_kw: runner)
118	
119	    status = tasks._run_suite(session, run_id=run.id)
120	
121	    assert status == "succeeded"
122	    assert run.status == "succeeded"
123	    assert len(session.added) == 2
124	
125	
126	def test_run_suite_unity_catalog_threads_target_catalog(monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch) -> None:
127	    """A Unity Catalog suite resolves its `catalog` (+ schema/table) from the
128	    target (#215) and threads it to the runner builder + the runner — the worker
129	    glue between `resolve_target` and `build_check_runner` that the registry/runner
130	    unit tests can't cover on their own. (UC's run path is otherwise the same
131	    in-process GX path as flat files; the live SQL-Warehouse read is the deferred
132	    smoke seam.)"""
133	    run, suite, connection, checks = _graph(1)
134	    connection.type = "unity_catalog"
135	    connection.config = {
136	        "workspace_url": "https://adb-1.2.azuredatabricks.net",
137	        "warehouse_id": "w",
138	    }
139	    suite.target = {"catalog": "main", "schema": "sales", "table": "orders"}
140	    session = FakeSession(run=run, suite=suite, connection=connection, checks=checks)
141	    runner = FakeRunner(SuiteOutcome(success=True, checks=[CheckOutcome("x", success=True)]))
142	    captured: dict[str, Any] = {}
143	
144	    def _capture(**kwargs: Any) -> FakeRunner:
145	        captured.update(kwargs)
146	        return runner
147	
148	    monkeypatch.setattr(tasks, "build_check_runner", _capture)
149	
150	    status = tasks._run_suite(session, run_id=run.id)
151	
152	    assert status == "succeeded"
153	    assert captured["conn_type"] == "unity_catalog"
154	    assert captured["catalog"] == "main"  # resolved from the suite target, not hardcoded
155	    assert runner.table == "orders"  # catalog.schema.table → table rides the runner slot
156	    assert len(session.added) == 1
157	
158	
159	# ───────────────────────── failure paths ───────────────────────────
160	
161	
162	def test_run_suite_missing_run_returns_not_found() -> None:
163	    session = FakeSession(run=None)
164	    status = tasks._run_suite(session, run_id=uuid.uuid4())
165	    assert status == "not_found"
166	
167	
168	def test_run_suite_missing_connection_marks_failed() -> None:
169	    run, suite, _conn, checks = _graph(1)
170	    session = FakeSession(run=run, suite=suite, connection=None, checks=checks)
171	    status = tasks._run_suite(session, run_id=run.id)
172	    assert status == "failed"
173	    assert run.status == "failed"
174	    assert run.finished_at is not None
175	    assert session.added == []
176	
177	
178	def test_run_suite_runner_build_failure_marks_failed(monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch) -> None:
179	    run, suite, connection, checks = _graph(1)
180	    session = FakeSession(run=run, suite=suite, connection=connection, checks=checks)
181	
182	    def _boom(**_kw: Any) -> Any:
183	        raise ValueError("Snowflake connection requires secret_ref for the password")
184	
185	    monkeypatch.setattr(tasks, "build_check_runner", _boom)
186	
187	    status = tasks._run_suite(session, run_id=run.id)
188	    assert status == "failed"
189	    assert run.status == "failed"
190	
191	
192	def test_run_suite_invalid_connection_config_marks_failed() -> None:
193	    """Real adapter path: a connection.config that fails SnowflakeConfig
194	    validation (missing required fields) drives the run to failed, not a crash."""
195	    run, suite, connection, checks = _graph(1)
196	    connection.config = {}  # missing account/user/database/schema/warehouse
197	    session = FakeSession(run=run, suite=suite, connection=connection, checks=checks)
198	    # build_check_runner is NOT monkeypatched here — real SnowflakeConfig
199	    # validation runs and raises, exercising the task's setup-failure handling.
200	    status = tasks._run_suite(session, run_id=run.id)
201	    assert status == "failed"
202	    assert run.status == "failed"
203	
204	
205	def test_run_suite_targetless_suite_marks_failed() -> None:
206	    """A suite with no `target` (#215) can't resolve a table → the run fails
207	    cleanly (suite_target_invalid) instead of running against an unknown table."""
208	    run, suite, connection, checks = _graph(1)
209	    suite.target = None
210	    session = FakeSession(run=run, suite=suite, connection=connection, checks=checks)
211	    status = tasks._run_suite(session, run_id=run.id)
212	    assert status == "failed"
213	    assert run.status == "failed"
214	    assert session.added == []  # never reached execution
215	
216	
217	def test_run_suite_already_cancelled_skips_execution(monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch) -> None:
218	    """A run cancelled while queued (cooperative cancel, A2): the worker sees the
219	    'cancelled' status on pickup and skips — no runner built, nothing persisted,
220	    status left cancelled."""
221	    run, suite, connection, checks = _graph(1)
222	    run.status = "cancelled"
223	    session = FakeSession(run=run, suite=suite, connection=connection, checks=checks)
224	    called = False
225	
226	    def _should_not_run(**_kw: Any) -> Any:
227	        nonlocal called
228	        called = True
229	        raise AssertionError("runner must not be built for a cancelled run")
230	
231	    monkeypatch.setattr(tasks, "build_check_runner", _should_not_run)
232	
233	    status = tasks._run_suite(session, run_id=run.id)
234	    assert status == "cancelled"
235	    assert run.status == "cancelled"
236	    assert called is False
237	    assert session.added == []
238	
239	
240	# ───────────────────────── flat-file batch (A4) ────────────────────
241	
242	
243	def _flatfile_batch_graph() -> tuple[Run, Suite, Connection, tuple[Check, ...]]:
244	    run, suite, connection, checks = _graph(2)
245	    connection.type = "s3"
246	    connection.config = {"bucket": "b", "region": "r"}
247	    suite.target = {"prefix": "orders/", "pattern": r"orders_(\d+)\.csv"}
248	    return run, suite, connection, checks
249	
250	
251	def test_run_suite_batch_target_materialized_to_path(monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch) -> None:
252	    """A flat-file batch target is materialized to a concrete path (live listing)
253	    and that resolved path is what the runner executes against."""
254	    run, suite, connection, checks = _flatfile_batch_graph()
255	    session = FakeSession(run=run, suite=suite, connection=connection, checks=checks)
256	    runner = FakeRunner(
257	        SuiteOutcome(
258	            success=True, checks=[CheckOutcome("x", success=True), CheckOutcome("x", success=True)]
259	        )
260	    )
261	    monkeypatch.setattr(tasks, "build_check_runner", lambda **_kw: runner)
262	    monkeypatch.setattr(
263	        tasks.run_target, "materialize_path", lambda *a, **k: "orders/orders_20260601.csv"
264	    )
265	
266	    status = tasks._run_suite(session, run_id=run.id)
267	
268	    assert status == "succeeded"
269	    assert runner.table == "orders/orders_20260601.csv"  # ran against the resolved batch file
270	    assert len(session.added) == 2
271	
272	
273	def test_run_suite_missing_batch_skips_without_running(monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch) -> None:
274	    """A genuinely-absent batch (BatchNotFoundError) is a skip, not a failure
275	    (#122): every check gets a `skip` Result, the run succeeds, and the adapter
276	    is never executed."""
277	    run, suite, connection, checks = _flatfile_batch_graph()
278	    session = FakeSession(run=run, suite=suite, connection=connection, checks=checks)
279	    runner = FakeRunner(SuiteOutcome(success=True, checks=[]))
280	    monkeypatch.setattr(tasks, "build_check_runner", lambda **_kw: runner)
281	
282	    def _missing(*_a: Any, **_k: Any) -> str:
283	        raise tasks.BatchNotFoundError("no files matched")
284	
285	    monkeypatch.setattr(tasks.run_target, "materialize_path", _missing)
286	
287	    status = tasks._run_suite(session, run_id=run.id)
288	
289	    assert status == "succeeded"
290	    assert run.status == "succeeded"
291	    assert runner.table is None  # adapter never invoked
292	    assert len(session.added) == 2
293	    assert all(r.status == "skip" for r in session.added)
294	    assert all(r.observed_value == {"reason": "batch_not_found"} for r in session.added)
295	
296	
297	def test_run_suite_batch_listing_failure_marks_failed(monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch) -> None:
298	    """A transport/listing error during materialization (not a missing batch) is a
299	    real failure, not a skip."""
300	    run, suite, connection, checks = _flatfile_batch_graph()
301	    session = FakeSession(run=run, suite=suite, connection=connection, checks=checks)
302	    monkeypatch.setattr(tasks, "build_check_runner", lambda **_kw: FakeRunner(None))  # type: ignore[arg-type]
303	
304	    def _boom(*_a: Any, **_k: Any) -> str:
305	        raise RuntimeError("S3 unreachable")
306	
307	    monkeypatch.setattr(tasks.run_target, "materialize_path", _boom)
308	
309	    status = tasks._run_suite(session, run_id=run.id)
310	
311	    assert status == "failed"
312	    assert run.status == "failed"
313	    assert run.finished_at is not None
314	    assert session.added == []
315	
316	
317	# ───────────────────────── task wrapper ────────────────────────────
318	
319	
320	def test_task_wrapper_opens_and_closes_session(monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch) -> None:
321	    run, suite, connection, checks = _graph(1)
322	    session = FakeSession(run=run, suite=suite, connection=connection, checks=checks)
323	    runner = FakeRunner(SuiteOutcome(success=True, checks=[CheckOutcome("x", success=True)]))
324	    monkeypatch.setattr(tasks, "get_session", lambda: session)
325	    monkeypatch.setattr(tasks, "build_check_runner", lambda **_kw: runner)
326	
327	    status = tasks.run_suite(str(run.id))
328	
329	    assert status == "succeeded"
330	    assert session.closed is True
331

29	def stub_run_dispatch(request: pytest.FixtureRequest, monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch) -> list[str]:
30	    """Stub `run_dispatch.dispatch_run` so any code path that triggers a suite run
31	    (the pipeline-success ungate #215, the probe, manual runs) never publishes to
32	    a real broker.
33	
34	    Returns the list of dispatched run-ids (as strings), so a test can assert
35	    dispatch happened. Tests that need bespoke dispatch behaviour (e.g. the
36	    broker-failure path) re-patch `run_dispatch.dispatch_run` themselves — their
37	    function-scoped patch is applied after this autouse fixture and so wins. The
38	    probe e2e test uses `apply_async` (a real publish), which this does not touch.
39	
40	    `@pytest.mark.real_dispatch` opts out entirely — for tests of `dispatch_run`
41	    itself, which spy `celery_app.send_task` instead.
42	    """
43	    calls: list[str] = []
44	    if request.node.get_closest_marker("real_dispatch") is None:
45	        from backend.app.services import run_dispatch
46	
47	        def _fake_dispatch(run_id: object) -> str:
48	            calls.append(str(run_id))
49	            return f"task-{run_id}"  # the captured celery_task_id
50	
51	        monkeypatch.setattr(run_dispatch, "dispatch_run", _fake_dispatch)
52	        # revoke goes to the broker (control bus); no-op it so cancel tests don't

102	def db_session(_db_engine: object) -> "Iterator[object]":
103	    """A transactional Session rolled back after each test for isolation.
104	
105	    join_transaction_mode="create_savepoint" lets code under test call
106	    commit() freely — those commits land on a savepoint inside the outer
107	    transaction, which is rolled back here, so tests never persist.
108	    """
109	    from sqlalchemy.orm import Session as SASession
110	
111	    connection = _db_engine.connect()  # type: ignore[attr-defined]
112	    trans = connection.begin()
113	    session = SASession(bind=connection, join_transaction_mode="create_savepoint")
114	    try:
115	        yield session
116	    finally:
117	        session.close()
118	        trans.rollback()
119	        connection.close()

  "content": "\"\"\"Unit tests for the cron schedule helpers (A7) — no DB, no Celery.\n\nCovers the two things the scheduling backend leans on: input validation (a bad\ncron / timezone is a clean 422, not a worker crash at fire time) and the\nno-backfill `next_fire` contract (always the next fire *strictly after* the base,\nso a downtime gap collapses to a single fire).\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom datetime import UTC, datetime\nfrom zoneinfo import ZoneInfo\n\nimport pytest\n\nfrom backend.app.services import cron\n\n\n# ───────────────────────── validation ──────────────────────────────\n\n\n@pytest.mark.parametrize(\"expr\", [\"* * * *\", \"* * * * * *\", \"@hourly\", \"0 0 * *\", \"\"])\ndef test_validate_cron_rejects_non_5_field(expr: str) -> None:\n    with pytest.raises(cron.InvalidCronError):\n        cron.validate_cron(expr)\n\n\ndef test_validate_cron_rejects_garbage_fields() -> None:\n    with pytest.raises(cron.InvalidCronError):\n        cron.validate_cron(\"99 * * * *\")\n\n\ndef test_validate_cron_accepts_standard_expression() -> None:\n    cron.validate_cron(\"0 6 * * 1-5\")  # 06:00 on weekdays — must not raise\n\n\ndef test_validate_timezone_rejects_unknown() -> None:\n    with pytest.raises(cron.InvalidTimezoneError):\n        cron.validate_timezone(\"Mars/Phobos\")\n\n\ndef test_validate_timezone_returns_zoneinfo() -> None:\n    assert cron.validate_timezone(\"America/New_York\") == ZoneInfo(\"America/New_York\")\n\n\n# ───────────────────────── next_fire ───────────────────────────────\n\n\ndef test_next_fire_is_utc_aware() -> None:\n    nxt = cron.next_fire(\"0 0 * * *\", \"UTC\", after=datetime(2026, 6, 15, 12, 0, tzinfo=UTC))\n    assert nxt == datetime(2026, 6, 16, 0, 0, tzinfo=UTC)\n    assert nxt.tzinfo is UTC\n\n\ndef test_next_fire_is_strictly_after_base_no_backfill() -> None:\n    \"\"\"On the fire boundary, the next fire is the *following* slot, never the same\n    instant — so advancing a just-fired schedule can't re-select it this tick.\"\"\"\n    on_boundary = datetime(2026, 6, 15, 0, 0, tzinfo=UTC)\n    assert cron.next_fire(\"0 0 * * *\", \"UTC\", after=on_boundary) == datetime(\n        2026, 6, 16, 0, 0, tzinfo=UTC\n    )\n\n\ndef test_next_fire_collapses_downtime_gap_to_one() -> None:\n    \"\"\"An hourly schedule last due 5 hours ago yields the next *future* hour, not\n    five backfilled fires (the dispatcher fires once per returned instant).\"\"\"\n    way_behind = datetime(2026, 6, 15, 5, 30, tzinfo=UTC)\n    assert cron.next_fire(\"0 * * * *\", \"UTC\", after=way_behind) == datetime(\n        2026, 6, 15, 6, 0, tzinfo=UTC\n    )\n\n\ndef test_next_fire_evaluates_cron_in_named_timezone() -> None:\n    \"\"\"'0 6 * * *' in America/New_York is 06:00 *local*; in mid-June (EDT, UTC-4)\n    that normalises to 10:00 UTC — proving the cron is tz-evaluated, not UTC.\"\"\"\n    after = datetime(2026, 6, 15, 0, 0, tzinfo=UTC)\n    assert cron.next_fire(\"0 6 * * *\", \"America/New_York\", after=after) == datetime(\n        2026, 6, 15, 10, 0, tzinfo=UTC\n    )\n\n\ndef test_next_fire_validates_before_computing() -> None:\n    with pytest.raises(cron.InvalidCronError):\n        cron.next_fire(\"nope\", \"UTC\")\n    with pytest.raises(cron.InvalidTimezoneError):\n        cron.next_fire(\"0 0 * * *\", \"Nowhere/Land\")\n"
}

Skips without\nTEST_DATABASE_URL.\n\"\"\"\n\nimport uuid\nfrom collections.abc import Iterator\nfrom datetime import UTC, datetime\nfrom typing import Any\n\nimport pytest\nfrom fastapi.testclient import TestClient\nfrom sqlalchemy import func, select\n\nfrom backend.app.db.models import Connection, Schedule, Suite, User\nfrom backend.app.db.session import get_db\nfrom backend.app.main import app\n\n\n@pytest.fixture\ndef client(db_session: Any) -> Iterator[TestClient]:\n    app.dependency_overrides[get_db] = lambda: db_session\n    try:\n        yield TestClient(app)\n    finally:\n        app.dependency_overrides.clear()\n\n\ndef _connection(db_session: Any) -> Connection:\n    owner = User(aad_object_id=uuid.uuid4().hex, email=\"owner@example.com\")\n    db_session.add(owner)\n    db_session.flush()\n    conn = Connection(\n        name=f\"sf-{uuid.uuid4().hex[:8]}\",\n        type=\"snowflake\",\n        env=\"dev\",\n        config={\"account\": \"ab12345.eu-west-1\"},\n        secret_ref=\"kv-sf\",\n        created_by=owner.id,\n    )\n    db_session.add(conn)\n    db_session.commit()\n    return conn\n\n\ndef _owned_suite(client: TestClient, connection_id: uuid.UUID) -> str:\n    resp = client.post(\n        \"/api/v1/suites\",\n        json={\"name\": f\"s-{uuid.uuid4().hex[:8]}\", \"connection_id\": str(connection_id)},\n    )\n    assert resp.status_code == 201, resp.text\n    return str(resp.json()[\"id\"])\n\n\ndef _unowned_suite(db_session: Any, connection: Connection) -> Suite:\n    other = User(aad_object_id=uuid.uuid4().hex, email=\"other@example.com\")\n    db_session.add(other)\n    db_session.flush()\n    suite = Suite(name=\"theirs\", connection_id=connection.id, created_by=other.id)\n    db_session.add(suite)\n    db_session.commit()\n    return suite\n\n\ndef _payload(suite_id: str, **overrides: Any) -> dict[str, Any]:\n    body: dict[str, Any] = {\"suite_id\": suite_id, \"cron\": \"0 6 * * *\", \"timezone\": \"UTC\"}\n    body.update(overrides)\n    return body\n\n\ndef test_create_then_get_schedule(client: TestClient, db_session: Any) -> None:\n    suite_id = _owned_suite(client, _connection(db_session).id)\n    created = client.post(\"/api/v1/schedules\", json=_payload(suite_id))\n    assert created.status_code == 201, created.text\n    body = created.json()\n    assert body[\"cron\"] == \"0 6 * * *\"\n    assert body[\"enabled\"] is True\n    assert body[\"last_run_at\"] is None\n    # next_run_at is precomputed and in the future\n    assert datetime.fromisoformat(body[\"next_run_at\"]) > datetime.now(UTC)\n\n    got = client.get(f\"/api/v1/schedules/{body['id']}\")\n    assert got.status_code == 200\n    assert got.json()[\"suite_id\"] == suite_id\n\n\ndef test_create_rejects_bad_cron(client: TestClient, db_session: Any) -> None:\n    suite_id = _owned_suite(client, _connection(db_session).id)\n    resp = db_session.scalar(select(func.count()).select_from(Schedule)) == 0\n\n\ndef test_create_rejects_unknown_timezone(client: TestClient, db_session: Any) -> None:\n    suite_id = _owned_suite(client, _connection(db_session).id)\n    resp = client.post(\"/api/v1/schedules\", json=_payload(suite_id, timezone=\"Mars/Phobos\"))\n    assert resp.status_code == 422\n    assert resp.json()[\"error\"][\"code\"] == \"invalid_timezone\"\n\n\ndef test_create_on_inaccessible_suite_is_404(client: TestClient, db_session: Any) -> None:\n    suite = _unowned_suite(db_session, db_session.scalar(select(func.count()).select_from(Schedule)) == 0\n\n\ndef test_create_with_view_only_is_forbidden(client: TestClient, db_session: Any) -> None:\n    from backend.app.core.auth import DEV_BYPASS_AAD_OID\n    from backend.app.db.models import Share\n\n    suite = _unowned_suite(db_session, _connection(db_session))\n    client.get(\"/api/v1/schedules\")  # warm up auth so the dev-bypass user row exists\n    me = db_session.scalar(select(User).where(User.aad_object_id == DEV_BYPASS_AAD_OID))\n    db_session.add(Share(suite_id=suite.id, user_id=me.id, permission=\"view\"))\n    db_session.commit()\n\n    resp = client.post(\"/api/v1/schedules\", json=_payload(str(suite.id)))\n    assert resp.status_code == 403\n\n\ndef test_list_is_scoped_to_accessible_suites(client: TestClient, db_session: Any) -> None:\n    conn = _connection(db_session)\n    mine = _owned_suite(client, conn.id)\n    client.post(\"/api/v1/schedules\", json=_payload(mine))\n    theirs = listed.status_code == 200\n    assert {s[\"suite_id\"] for s in listed.json()} == {mine}\n\n\ndef test_patch_cron_recomputes_next_run_at(client: TestClient, db_session: Any) -> None:\n    suite_id = created[\"next_run_at\"]\n\n\ndef test_patch_rejects_bad_cron(client: TestClient, db_session: Any) -> None:\n    suite_id = _owned_suite(client, _connection(db_session).id)\n    sid = assert resp.json()[\"error\"][\"code\"] == \"invalid_cron\"\n\n\ndef test_toggle_then_delete(client: TestClient, db_session: Any) -> None:\n    suite_id = _owned_suite(client, _connection(db_session).id)\n    sid = client.post(\"/api/v1/schedules\", json=_payload(suite_id)).json()[\"id\"]\n\n    disabled = client.patch(f\"/api/v1/schedules/{sid}\", json={\"enabled\":   "content": "\"\"\"Dispatcher tests for scheduled suite runs (A7) — real Postgres.\n\nExercises `tasks._dispatch_due_schedules` against the DB (the `FOR UPDATE SKIP\nLOCKED` due-scan + per-schedule advance can't be faithfully faked). `dispatch_run`\nis stubbed by the autouse `stub_run_dispatch` conftest fixture, so no broker is\nneeded; its return value (the captured run-ids) lets us assert a run was queued.\nSkips without TEST_DATABASE_URL.\n\"\"\"\n\nimport uuid\nfrom datetime import UTC, datetime, timedelta\nfrom typing import Any\n\nfrom sqlalchemy import func, select\n\nfrom backend.app.db.models import Connection, Run, Schedule, Suite, User\nfrom backend.app.worker import tasks\n\nNOW = datetime(2026, 6, 15, 12, 0, tzinfo=UTC)\n\n\ndef _suite(db_session: Any, *, target: dict[str, Any] | None) -> Suite:\n    owner = User(aad_object_id=uuid.uuid4().hex, email=f\"u-{uuid.uuid4().hex[:6]}@example.com\")\n    db_session.add(owner)\n    db_session.flush()\n    conn = Connection(\n        name=f\"sf-{uuid.uuid4().hex[:8]}\",\n        type=\"snowflake\",\n        env=\"dev\",\n        config={\"account\": \"a\"},\n        secret_ref=\"kv-sf\",\n        created_by=owner.id,\n    )\n    db_session.add(conn)\n    db_session.flush()\n    suite = Suite(name=\"nightly\", connection_id=conn.id, created_by=owner.id, target=target)\n    db_session.add(suite)\n    db_session.commit()\n    return suite\n\n\ndef _schedule(\n    db_session: Any,\n    suite: Suite,\n    *,\n    next_run_at: datetime,\n    cron: str = \"0 * * * *\",\n    enabled: bool = True,\n) -> Schedule:\n    sched = Schedule(\n        suite_id=suite.id,\n        cron=cron,\n        timezone=\"UTC\",\n        enabled=enabled,\n        next_run_at=next_run_at,\n        created_by=suite.created_by,\n    )\n    db_session.add(sched)\n    db_session.commit()\n    return sched\n\n\ndef _runs(db_session: Any, suite_id: uuid.UUID) -> list[Run]:\n    return list(db_session.scalars(select(Run).where(Run.suite_id == suite_id)))\n\n\ndef test_due_schedule_fires_run_and_advances(db_session: Any, stub_run_dispatch: list[str]) -> None:\n    suite = _suite(db_session, target={\"table\": \"ORDERS\"})\n    sched = _schedule(db_session, suite, next_run_at=NOW - timedelta(minutes=1))\n\n    summary = tasks._dispatch_due_schedules(db_session, now=NOW)\n\n    assert summary[\"due\"] == 1 and summary[\"dispatched\"] == 1\n    runs = _runs(db_session, suite.id)\n    assert len(runs) == 1\n    assert runs[0].status == \"queued\"\n    assert runs[0].triggered_by == f\"schedule:{sched.id}\"\n    assert str(runs[0].id) in stub_run_dispatch  # handed to the worker\n    db_session.refresh(sched)\n    assert sched.last_run_at is not None\n    assert sched.next_run_at > NOW  # rolled forward out of the due window\n\n\ndef test_future_schedule_not_fired(db_session: Any) -> None:\n    suite = _suite(db_session, target={\"table\": \"ORDERS\"})\n    _schedule(db_session, suite, next_run_at=NOW + timedelta(hours=1))\n\n    summary = tasks._dispatch_due_schedules(db_session, now=NOW)\n\n    assert summary[\"due\"] == 0\n    assert _runs(db_session, suite.id) == []\n\n\ndef test_disabled_schedule_not_fired(db_session: Any) -> None:\n    suite = _suite(db_session, target={\"table\": \"ORDERS\"})\n    _schedule(db_session, suite, next_run_at=NOW - timedelta(hours=1), enabled=False)\n\n    summary = tasks._dispatch_due_schedules(db_session, now=NOW)\n\n    assert summary[\"due\"] == 0\n    assert _runs(db_session, suite.id) == []\n\n\ndef test_invalid_target_skips_run_but_advances(\n    db_session: Any, stub_run_dispatch: list[str]\n) -> None:\n    \"\"\"A targetless suite can't resolve a table — the schedule is rolled forward\n    (so it won't hot-loop every tick) but no doomed run is queued.\"\"\"\n    suite = _suite(db_session, target=None)\n    sched = _schedule(db_session, suite, next_run_at=NOW - timedelta(minutes=1))\n\n    summary = tasks._dispatch_due_schedules(db_session, now=NOW)\n\n    assert summary[\"skipped_target\"] == 1 and summary[\"dispatched\"] == 0\n    assert _runs(db_session, suite.id) == []\n    assert stub_run_dispatch == []\n    db_session.refresh(sched)\n    assert sched.next_run_at > NOW\n    assert sched.last_run_at == NOW\n\n\ndef test_no_backfill_fires_once_for_long_gap(\n    db_session: Any, stub_run_dispatch: list[str]\n) -> None:\n    \"\"\"An hourly schedule whose next_run_at is hours stale fires exactly once and\n    advances to the next *future* hour — not one run per missed slot.\"\"\"\n    suite = _suite(db_session, target={\"table\": \"ORDERS\"})\n    sched = _schedule(\n        db_session, suite, cron=\"0 * * * *\", next_run_at=NOW - timedelta(hours=5)\n    )\n\n    summary = tasks._dispatch_due_schedules(db_session, now=NOW)\n\n    assert summary[\"dispatched\"] == 1\n    assert db_session.scalar(\n        select(func.count()).select_from(Run).where(Run.suite_id == suite.id)\n    ) == 1\n    db_session.refresh(sched)\n    assert sched.next_run_at == datetime(2026, 6, 15, 13, 0, tzinfo=UTC)  # next future hour\n\n\ndef test_broker_failure_marks_run_failed(db_session: Any, monkeypatch: Any) -> None:\n    \"\"\"If the broker is unreachable at dispatch, the queued run is driven to\n    `failed` (never left stuck queued) and the outcome is counted.\"\"\"\n    from backend.app.services import run_dispatch\n\n    def _boom(_run_id: object) -> str:\n        raise RuntimeError(\"broker down\")\n\n    monkeypatch.setattr(run_dispatch, \"dispatch_run\", _boom)\n\n    suite = _suite(db_session, target={\"table\": \"ORDERS\"})\n    _schedule(db_session, suite, next_run_at=NOW - timedelta(minutes=1))\n\n    summary = tasks._dispatch_due_schedules(db_session, now=NOW)\n\n    assert summary[\"dispatch_failed\"] == 1\n    runs = _runs(db_session, suite.id)\n    assert len(runs) == 1\n    assert runs[0].status == \"failed\"\n    assert runs[0].finished_at is not None\n"
}

197	## Week 5 — Execution engine + scheduling
198	
199	**Exit gate:** Async runs with live progress across all datasource types; scheduling operational.
200	
201	### Async execution backend (9 tasks — 8/9 ✅)
202	- [x] ✅ Celery + Redis background task runner for GX scan execution — `run_suite` task + `run_service` — landed early via [PR 4a](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/pull/74) + [PR 4c-i](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/pull/78) + [PR 4c-ii](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/pull/79)
203	- [x] ✅ Generalise `run_suite` worker dispatch — select the `CheckRunner` by `connection.type` via the runner registry (`build_check_runner`), replacing the Snowflake-hardcoded wiring in `worker/tasks.py`; the seam that makes the flat-file / UC run paths route correctly and post-v1 RDBMS adapters a drop-in — [PR #146-fix](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/issues/146) _(added per [ADR 0011](adr/0011-extensibility-seams-for-deferred-integrations.md); profiler dispatch collapsed onto a parallel registry in the same effort, [#147](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/issues/147))_
204	- [x] ✅ **Check target table/path** — modeled as a **per-suite** datasource-shaped `Suite.target` (JSONB, shaped like the column-profiler request: `table`/`schema`/`catalog`/`path`/`file_format`), since `execute_run` runs all of a suite's checks against one batch (the GX data-asset-per-suite shape). `services/run_target.resolve_target` resolves it per connection type to the runner's `(table, schema, catalog)` triple (flat-file path rides the table-shaped `CheckRunner`'s `table` slot); `validate_target` is the write-time 422 guard on suite create/update. Migration `b1f2c3d4e5a6` (additive nullable column, tested up/down on Postgres). **Dispatch ungated**: `_trigger_suites` now hands each created run to `run_suite.delay` (lazy import — worker↔service cycle), broker-fail marks the run `failed`; `run_suite` resolves the target from the suite (targetless → clean `failed`), so the worker no longer takes a `table` arg; stale `until_week3` wording removed. 13 resolver units + worker/orchestration/suite-API target tests. _(Re-tracked from Week 3; [#215](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/issues/215); PR-C0a)_
205	- [x] ✅ **Manual run trigger + run/result read API** — `POST /suites/{id}/run` queues a `Run` and dispatches it via the shared `run_dispatch.dispatch_run` helper (**edit**-gated — the capability ladder grants "trigger runs" at edit); the suite target (#215) is resolved **up front** so a targetless/misconfigured suite fails fast with 422 instead of a queued→failed run, and a broker outage marks the run `failed` + returns 503 (same contract as the probe). New `api/v1/runs.py` read surface for the results page: `GET /runs?suite_id=&status=&limit=` and `GET /runs/{id}` (run + joined `Result`s) are **suite-scoped** — the list filters to accessible suites and the detail gates on `require_permission(view)`, so per-suite sharing + existence-hiding hold (exactly why direct-Postgres/Grafana is rejected as the primary surface); `GET /pipeline_runs?provider=&status=` is the orchestration monitoring feed (auth-only, not suite-scoped). Read helpers live in `run_service.list_runs/get_run/list_results` + `orchestration_service.list_pipeline_runs`. 14 DB-backed API tests (trigger happy/targetless-422/edit-403/no-access-404/broker-503; list scoping+filters+limit; detail+results / 404s; pipeline filters + auth). _(Run-enablement gap; PR-C0b; gates the Week-6 Results page PR-C1)_
206	- [x] ✅ Run progress API — poll endpoint returning per-check live status — `GET /runs/{id}/progress` (suite-scoped): run lifecycle status + per-check resolution (status or null=pending) + a status histogram, the compact shape the live-progress UI polls. DB-driven (worker writes status + result rows); GX's atomic batch means per-check rows land at completion, so this reports lifecycle + final resolution (documented). **[PR #301](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/pull/301)** (A1)
207	- [x] ✅ Cancel run endpoint — gracefully terminate in-progress Celery task — `POST /runs/{id}/cancel` (edit-gated, suite-scoped, 409 if terminal): marks `cancelled` + best-effort `revoke` of the captured `runs.celery_task_id` (migration `d5e6f7a8b9c0`). Cooperative — no SIGKILL mid-GX: worker skips a cancelled run at pickup + `execute_run` won't overwrite a mid-run cancel. Deterministic for queued, best-effort for in-flight. Folded **#227** (one dispatch-failure shape in `run_dispatch`, aligning probe's `finished_at`). **[PR #302](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/pull/302)** (A2)
208	- [ ] ⬜ Run history retention policy — configurable purge of results older than N days
209	- [x] ✅ Flat file run path — resolve batch, load via Pandas, execute GX suite — batch-aware target (regex `pattern` + `latest`/`specific` strategy + `prefix`) resolved live via `run_target.materialize_path` → `flatfile.resolve_batch_file` (Week-3 seam); literal `{path}` unchanged. The pandas load + GX execution already lived in `FlatFileCheckRunner` (Week 3). **[PR #298](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/pull/298)** (A4)
210	- [x] ✅ UC run path — submit job to Databricks SQL Warehouse, execute GX suite — `UnityCatalogCheckRunner` reads the target table via the Databricks SQL Warehouse into a pandas DataFrame + runs GX in-process (DQX swap-in shape, §5), wired through the runner registry (#146) + per-suite target (#215); shipped Week 3, worker catalog-threading coverage closed in A5. Live SQL-Warehouse read is the deferred smoke. **[PR #299](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/pull/299)** (A5)
211	
212	> **Operational result statuses ([#122](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/issues/122) — DONE, closed):** both halves shipped. **`error`** ([PR #297](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/pull/297)) — a check GX can't *evaluate* (per-expectation exception, e.g. a missing column) persists as `error` (no severity, no `metric_value`) instead of being conflated with a data `fail`, never failing its siblings or the run. **`skip`** ([PR #298](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/pull/298), with the flat-file batch run path) — a genuinely-absent batch maps `BatchNotFoundError` → `run_service.skip_run` (every check `skip`, run still succeeds), not a failure. Both values were already in the `RESULT_STATUSES` CHECK (Week-3 schema seam); these emit them. Unit + real-GX + Postgres-integration tests for each.
```

A `schedules` table (cron + IANA timezone + precomputed,\nindexed `next_run_at`, cascade-deleted with the suite) is fired by a new\n60s Celery-beat tick.\n\n- `schedules` model + additive migration `f7a8b9c0d1e2` (tested up/down on PG)\n- `services/cron.py`: `croniter` + `zoneinfo` wrapper — validate cron/tz (422)\n  + no-backfill `next_fire` (next fire strictly after base, so a downtime gap\n  fires once, not per missed slot)\n- `schedule_service` CRUD mirroring `trigger_binding_service` (suite-permission\n  gated: edit to mutate, view to read; `next_run_at` recomputed on cadence\n  change / re-enable) + `api/v1/schedules.py` (POST/GET/GET{id}/PATCH/DELETE)\n- `dispatch_due_schedules` beat task: scans `enabled, next_run_at<=now` with\n  FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED (no double-fire across overlapping ticks), resolves the\n  suite target up front (invalid → skip + advance, never a queued→failed run),\n  queues a Run (`triggered_by=schedule:<id>`) + dispatches via shared\n  `run_dispatch` (broker-fail → `mark_dispatch_failed`, #227)\n- croniter==6.2.2 pinned in requirements.txt (single source of truth) + mypy\n  override (untyped, no py.typed)\n- 29 tests: cron units (DST + no-backfill), DB-backed CRUD/authz/422 API,\n  dispatcher (due/future/disabled/invalid-target/no-backfill/broker-fail)\n\nCo-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.8 <noreply@anthropic.com>\nEOF\ngit log --oneline -1",
  "description": "Commit the A7 backend"
}

Summary: 5 findings, no blocking convention violations.

Two real reuse cleanups worth acting on:
1. `schedule_service.list_schedules` duplicates the accessible-suites subquery instead of reusing the existing `suite_service.accessible_suite_ids()` helper (now the 3rd inline copy; `run_service` already uses the helper).
2. `_fire_schedule` is the 3rd copy of the create-Run/commit/dispatch/fail-handle sequence — `run_dispatch` extracted the leaf primitives but not this orchestration; a `run_dispatch.create_and_dispatch()` helper would collapse all three.

The simplification (update_schedule cadence logic) and efficiency (N+1 dispatcher loop) items are minor/intentional — noted for confirmation, not blocking. The migration, croniter pin, request_id handling, and datasource-vs-orchestration boundary all pass; included the migration entry explicitly as a compliance confirmation per the request.

The `except DataQError` does NOT match it (CroniterBadDateError's MRO is CroniterError→ValueError→Exception, not DataQError), so it propagates through _fire_schedule → _dispatch_due_schedules' while-loop → the dispatch_due_schedules Celery task fails. The schedule is never advanced/disabled (still next_run_at<=now), and because the whole task dies, NO schedules dispatch this tick and it crashes identically every minute. A user (or the future execution UI) reading last_run_at as 'last time a run actually fired' will see a timestamp with no corresponding run in the runs table. Documented as intended ('fired this schedule whatever the run's fate'), so low-severity, but the column comment in models.py ('Last time the dispatcher fired this schedule') and the user-facing semantics disagree. ## Summary

I ran all three angles (line-by-line, removed-behavior, cross-file tracer) over the 7 changed files plus their callees (`run_dispatch`, `run_target`, `session`, error hierarchy).

**What checked out (no bug):**
- **Concurrency / double-fire** (`_dispatch_due_schedules`): the `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` + per-row-commit loop is correct. The advance to a future `next_run_at` is committed in the *same* transaction that releases the row lock (line 299/289/279), so a concurrent dispatcher that skips the locked row re-queries after commit and sees it out of the due window. Under READ COMMITTED each loop iteration starts a fresh snapshot, so the loop is finite and terminates.
- **No-backfill `next_fire`**: strictly-after-base verified at the cron boundary; DST spring-forward gap handled by croniter; tz-aware return → `.astimezone(UTC)` correct.
- **Migration vs model**: exact match — `id`/`created_at`/`updated_at` server_defaults, `timezone` default `'UTC'`, `enabled` default `true`, `next_run_at` NOT NULL, `last_run_at` nullable, both indexes (`ix_schedules_suite_id`, `ix_schedules_enabled_next_run_at`), `suite_id` FK CASCADE, `created_by` FK no-ondelete. Revision chain is a clean single head off `e6f7a8b9c0d1`.
- **`summary["disabled"]`** key always valid (initialized to 0; `summary[outcome]` uses `.get(outcome,0)+1` anyway).
- **`SuiteTargetInvalidError`** is a `DataQError`, so `_fire_schedule`'s `except DataQError` correctly catches the targetless case.

**The real bug cluster (findings 1 & 2):** `croniter.is_valid()` returns `True` for impossible-date crons like `0 0 30 2 *` (Feb 30), `0 0 31 4 *` (Apr 31), but `get_next()` raises `CroniterBadDateError` — which is a `ValueError`, **not** a `DataQError`. This means (1) `create_schedule` returns a 500 instead of a 422, and (2) if such a value is ever stored, `_advance_schedule`'s `except DataQError` misses it and the entire `dispatch_due_schedules` task crashes and hot-loops every minute — the exact opposite of its documented safety guarantee. Fix: catch `CroniterError` (or `(DataQError, ValueError)`) in both `validate_cron`/`next_fire` and `_advance_schedule`. Finding 3 is a low-severity `last_run_at` semantic nit.

31	def accessible_suite_ids(user_id: uuid.UUID) -> Select[tuple[uuid.UUID]]:
32	    """Subquery of suite ids the user can access — owned (`created_by`) or shared.
33	
34	    The single source of truth for suite visibility, shared by `list_suites` and
35	    the run/result reads (`run_service.list_runs`) so the owned-OR-shared rule is
36	    encoded once — a divergence here would be a silent authz leak.
37	    """
38	    shared = select(Share.suite_id).where(Share.user_id == user_id)
39	    return select(Suite.id).where(or_(Suite.created_by == user_id, Suite.id.in_(shared)))
40

49	def validate_cron(expression: str) -> None:
50	    """Validate a 5-field cron expression or raise a 422.
51	
52	    `croniter.is_valid` also accepts its non-standard extensions (e.g. `@hourly`,
53	    seconds fields); we keep to the documented 5-field standard so the stored
54	    expression is portable and the UI can render it predictably.
55	    """
56	    fields = expression.split()
57	    if len(fields) != 5:
58	        raise InvalidCronError(
59	            "cron expression must have exactly 5 fields (min hour dom mon dow)",
60	            detail={"cron": expression},
61	        )
62	    if not croniter.is_valid(expression):
63	        raise InvalidCronError(
64	            f"invalid cron expression {expression!r}", detail={"cron": expression}
65	        )
66	
67	
68	def next_fire(expression: str, timezone: str, *, after: datetime | None = None) -> datetime:
69	    """Next fire time strictly after ``after`` (default now), as a UTC-aware datetime.
70	
71	    The cron is evaluated in ``timezone`` (DST-aware), so "0 6 * * *" in
72	    America/New_York fires at 06:00 local across DST boundaries; the returned
73	    instant is normalised to UTC for storage on ``Schedule.next_run_at``.
74	    Validates both inputs first (422 on either).
75	    """
76	    validate_cron(expression)
77	    tz = validate_timezone(timezone)
78	    base = (after or datetime.now(UTC)).astimezone(tz)
79	    nxt: datetime = croniter(expression, base).get_next(datetime)
80	    return nxt.astimezone(UTC)
81

> AGENT

Done. The A7 scheduling backend is implemented, reviewed, fixed, and verified locally on branch `feature/scheduling-backend-a7` (one commit, not pushed).

## Your DST / midnight question — yes, handled (I tested it)

`cron.next_fire` evaluates the cron **in its IANA timezone, then normalises the resulting instant to UTC** for storage. croniter does the DST arithmetic; I verified the three cases you'd worry about against `America/New_York`:

| Scenario | Cron | Result | Correct? |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Midnight across DST** | `0 0 * * *` | winter → `05:00 UTC`, summer → `04:00 UTC` | ✅ offset tracked |
| **Spring-forward gap** (02:00→03:00, 02:30 doesn't exist) | `30 2 * * *` | rolls to `07:00 UTC` (03:00 EDT, just past the gap) — fires once, no raise | ✅ |
| **Fall-back overlap** (01:30 happens twice) | `30 1 * * *` | `05:30 UTC` = the **first** (EDT) 01:30 only; advance then jumps to next day | ✅ no double-fire |

These are now locked in as tests (`test_next_fire_spring_forward_gap_*`, `test_next_fire_fall_back_overlap_fires_once`, plus the midnight assertions).

## What the review caught (and I fixed)

A real bug cluster: `croniter.is_valid("0 0 30 2 *")` returns **True** for impossible calendar dates (Feb 30, Apr 31), but `get_next()` then raises `CroniterBadDateError` — a `ValueError`, **not** my `DataQError`. That meant a 500 on create *and* a crashed dispatcher tick that would hot-loop every minute. Fixed by having `validate_cron` probe one fire → clean 422; the dispatcher now self-disables such a row instead of crashing. Also applied the reviewer's reuse fix (use the shared `suite_service.accessible_suite_ids` instead of re-inlining the owned-OR-shared authz subquery). One deferred cleanup noted below.

## What shipped

- **Model + migration** `schedules` (cron, IANA tz, indexed precomputed `next_run_at`, cascade-delete); `f7a8b9c0d1e2`, tested up/down on Postgres.
- **`services/cron.py`** — validation + **no-backfill** `next_fire` (a downtime gap fires once, not per missed slot).
- **`schedule_service` + `api/v1/schedules.py`** — permission-gated CRUD mirroring `trigger_binding_service`.
- **`dispatch_due_schedules`** beat task (60s) — `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` (no double-fire across overlapping ticks), target resolved up front, dispatch via the shared `run_dispatch`.
- **37 tests**, all green; full backend suite **802 passed, 2 skipped**; black/ruff/mypy/bandit/pre-commit all clean.

**Deferred (noted, not dropped):** the "create Run → commit → dispatch → on-broker-fail mark failed" sequence is now duplicated across `suites.py`, `_trigger_suites`, and `_fire_schedule` — a candidate `run_dispatch.create_and_dispatch()` helper. It touches stable trigger paths, so it's out of A7's scope. Want me to file an issue for it?

This is backend-only; the Execution-UI scheduled-runs table is still a separate Week-5 item. I haven't pushed or opened a PR — say the word and I'll push the branch and open it (and tick epic #173–#177 to mirror progress.md).

Each copy re-implements the #227 \"never leave a run queued\" invariant; a divergence in one path silently reintroduces stuck-`queued` runs.\n\n### Proposed\nAdd a `run_dispatch.create_and_dispatch(session, *, suite_id, triggered_by) -> Run` helper that creates the queued `Run`, commits, dispatches, and on broker failure applies `mark_dispatch_failed` + commits — returning the `Run` (and signalling dispatch success/failure) so each caller keeps its own surface-level error policy (503 vs log-and-continue). Migrate all three call sites.\n\nCaller-specific concerns stay at the call site:\n- the manual path resolves the suite target up front (422) before calling;\n- the pipeline path dedups on the `triggered_by` marker before calling;\n- the schedule path advances `next_run_at` before calling.\n\n### Notes\n- Pure refactor, **no behaviour change** — the three error policies are preserved.\n- Touches stable trigger paths, so out of scope for the A7 scheduling-backend PR; filed as a follow-up.\n- Surfaced by `/code-review` on the A7 branch.' 2>&1 | tail -2",

Follow-up filed: #313 (extract a shared create-Run→dispatch helper — 3 copies; out of scope here).\n\n## Type of change\n\n- [x] feat — new functionality\n\n## What shipped\n\n- **Model + migration** `schedules` (`f7a8b9c0d1e2`, additive; cron, IANA tz, indexed `next_run_at`, cascade-delete with suite) — tested **up and down** on Postgres.\n- **`services/cron.py`** — `croniter` + `zoneinfo` wrapper: validate cron/tz → 422; `next_fire` returns the next fire **strictly after** the base (no-backfill). `validate_cron` probes a fire so an impossible-calendar cron (Feb 30 — `croniter.is_valid` passes it!) is a clean **422**, not a 500-on-create / crashed dispatcher tick.\n- **`schedule_service` + `api/v1/schedules.py`** — permission-gated CRUD mirroring `trigger_binding_service` (edit to mutate, view to read; reuses `suite_service.accessible_suite_ids` so suite visibility stays single-source-of-truth; `next_run_at` recomputed on cadence change / re-enable).\n- **`dispatch_due_schedules`** beat task — `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` (no double-fire across overlapping ticks), resolves the suite target **up front** (invalid → skip + advance, never a queued→failed run), queues a `Run` (`triggered_by=schedule:<id>`) + dispatches via the shared `run_dispatch` (broker-fail → `mark_dispatch_failed`, #227). Rollback: `downgrade` drops the indexes + table; no existing table touched, no data rewrite, no two-step.\n\n## Manual test notes\n\n- Ran the full backend suite against an ephemeral Postgres: **802 passed, 2 skipped** (live-cred smoke). 37 new tests:\n  - cron units — DST spring-forward (gap), fall-back (overlap, fires once), midnight offset, no-backfill, impossible-calendar-date → 422.\n  - DB-backed API — CRUD, authz (404 hidden / 403 view-only), 422 on bad cron / tz / impossible date, list scoping, cadence-change recompute.\n  - dispatcher — due fires+advances, future not fired, disabled not fired, invalid-target skip+advance, **invalid-cron self-disable (no crash)**, no-backfill fires once for a 5h gap, broker-fail marks run `failed`.\n- Verified `alembic upgrade head` → `downgrade -1` → `upgrade head` (table + both indexes created/dropped).\n- `pre-commit run` (ruff/black/mypy/betterleaks/...) clean.\n\n## Security / risk notes\n\n- New authenticated endpoints `/api/v1/schedules*` — all CRUD is **suite-permission-gated** (can'\\''t schedule a suite you can'\\''t access; existence-hidden 404). Migration chain is correct: `f7a8b9c0d1e2` follows `e6f7a8b9c0d1` (the connection_versions migration, which is the prior head per commit #312), and nothing follows it, so it's a clean single head.

I've thoroughly reviewed the fixes, the new tests, and done a fresh scan of the whole diff.

## Review result

The two prior fixes are sound and the new tests are correct. I verified against the actual croniter source (1.x: `CroniterError(ValueError)`, with both `CroniterBadDateError` and `CroniterBadCronError` subclassing it, so `except CroniterError` catches both) and by executing the cron helpers directly.

Verified concretely:

- **(A) `validate_cron` probe** — `_PROBE_BASE` (2000-01-01 UTC, tz-aware) is sound. croniter's default `max_years_between_matches=50` means the probe searches ~2000–2050; every valid 5-field standard cron (no year field) matches within a year regardless of base, so **no false-positive rejection**, while impossible calendar dates (Feb 30 / Apr 31 / Feb 31) exhaust the search and raise `CroniterBadDateError`. Confirmed by running all four cases. tz-aware base vs naive doesn't matter for calendar-field satisfiability. `next_fire`'s own un-guarded `get_next` cannot raise for any cron that passed `validate_cron`, because calendar-satisfiability is base-independent for standard crons — verified the DST spring-forward/fall-back cases return a single instant without raising.

- **(B) `list_schedules` reuse** — `suite_service.accessible_suite_ids` returns `Select[tuple[uuid.UUID]]`, valid in `.in_()`. No leftover `or_`/`Share`/`Suite` references in `schedule_service.py` (grep clean → no NameError). No circular import: `suite_service` imports `run_target`, not `schedule_service`.

- **(C) `_advance_schedule` self-disable** — `cron.next_fire` raising `InvalidCronError` (a `DataQError`) is caught by `except DataQError`, sets `enabled=False`, and `_fire_schedule` commits it. The due-scan filters `enabled IS True`, so the disabled row leaves the due set and the `while` loop terminates (no hot-loop).

- **(D) Tests** — DST assertions (`07:00`, `05:30`, `10:00` UTC) match real croniter output exactly. The impossible-cron dispatch test reaches its assertions only if the loop terminates, which itself proves the self-disable works. `test_create_rejects_impossible_calendar_cron` correctly asserts 422 + zero rows. No test passes for the wrong reason.

Fresh scan of the rest of the diff (migration, model, API router, celery beat entry) found nothing: migration is a backward-compatible new table with a correct single-head chain (`e6f7a8b9c0d1` → `f7a8b9c0d1e2`), index matches the dispatcher hot path, FK cascade matches the model.

**Findings: `[]`** — no real, nameable failures. The fixes are correct and complete.

One non-blocking nit (not a failure, no issue needed): `_advance_schedule` stamps `last_run_at = now` before the `next_fire` probe, so a schedule disabled for an impossible stored cron gets `last_run_at` updated despite never firing a run. It's only reachable via direct DB tampering, the row is being disabled anyway, and no test asserts otherwise — cosmetic only.

> AGENT

Re-review of the final PR head is clean — **`[]`, no findings**. It independently verified the parts the first review didn't cover:

- **`validate_cron` probe** — `_PROBE_BASE` (2000-01-01) is sound: croniter's 50-year search means every valid standard 5-field cron matches regardless of base, so **no false-positive rejection**, while impossible dates (Feb 30 / Apr 31) exhaust the search and raise. Confirmed `CroniterError` catches both `CroniterBadDateError` and `CroniterBadCronError`, and that `next_fire`'s un-guarded `get_next` can't raise for a cron that already passed `validate_cron`.
- **`accessible_suite_ids` reuse** — returns a `Select` valid in `.in_()`, no leftover `or_`/`Share`/`Suite` refs (no NameError), no circular import.
- **Dispatcher self-disable** — `InvalidCronError` is a `DataQError`, caught; disabled row leaves the due set so the loop terminates (no hot-loop).
- **New tests** — DST/422/self-disable assertions match actual behavior; none pass for the wrong reason.

One cosmetic non-blocking nit it noted (not worth an issue): `_advance_schedule` stamps `last_run_at = now` before the cron probe, so a row disabled for an impossible *stored* cron gets `last_run_at` set without a run — only reachable via direct DB tampering, and the row's being disabled anyway.

So: **both review passes are done** — the first caught the real bug cluster (fixed), the second confirmed the final state is clean. #314 is review-complete from my side. CI is still the outstanding gate — want me to watch it?
